Regenerate them after changing any node's inputs, outputs or source header:
`<comfy_env python> tools/c2c_node_reference.py --pack ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks` (C2C workspace; with
`C2C_MAGNIFIC_KEEP_CNP=1` so the Magnific sign-in nodes are included even where OmniScale is installed).

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/AI Image


### NanoBananaC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Nano Banana · Gemini Image (C2C)

Generate or edit images with Google's Nano Banana family (gemini-3-pro-image / gemini-2.5-flash-image). Modes: text-to-image, edit, multi-image compose, style transfer. Five prompting styles (raw / cinematic / structured JSON / character-consistency / product shot). Wire up to 4 IMAGE inputs as references (batches flatten, max 14). Needs a Google AI Studio key: GEMINI_API_KEY in the C2C key store or the environment.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/nano_banana.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prompt` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | What to generate / how to edit. With prompt_style=structured_json you may paste a full JSON brief. |
| `model` | choice: `gemini-3-pro-image-preview`, `gemini-2.5-flash-image`, `gemini-2.5-flash-image-preview`, `gemini-2.0-flash-preview-image-generation` | default `"gemini-3-pro-image-preview"` | gemini-3-pro-image-preview = Nano Banana Pro (1K/2K/4K, best text, 14 refs). gemini-2.5-flash-image = fast GA model. |
| `mode` | choice: `text_to_image`, `edit_image`, `compose_images`, `style_transfer` | default `"text_to_image"` | edit_image needs ≥1 reference image; style_transfer needs ≥2 (first = content, last = style). |
| `prompt_style` | choice: `raw`, `cinematic`, `structured_json`, `character_consistency`, `product_shot` | default `"raw"` | Wraps your prompt in a proven template: cinematic photo, structured JSON brief, character-consistency lock, or commercial product shot. |
| `aspect_ratio` | choice: `auto`, `1:1`, `16:9`, `9:16`, `4:3`, `3:4`, `21:9`, `3:2`, … (+3) | default `"auto"` | Shape of the generated image. auto = the model decides (for edits it usually keeps the reference's shape). |
| `resolution` | choice: `auto`, `1K`, `2K`, `4K` | default `"auto"` | 1K/2K/4K are Nano Banana **Pro** only; flash models ignore this. |
| `enhance_prompt` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Higgsfield-style enhancer: a fast Gemini text pass expands your prompt into a rich, detailed image brief before generation. |
| `response_modalities` | choice: `IMAGE+TEXT`, `IMAGE` | default `"IMAGE+TEXT"` | IMAGE = picture only; IMAGE+TEXT also returns the model's commentary on the text_response output. |
| `temperature` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.05 | Randomness of the model. Lower = closer to the prompt and more repeatable, higher = more varied. |
| `batch_count` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…4 | Sequential API calls; results are stacked into one batch. |
| `seed` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…2147483647 | Gemini has no true seed — this controls ComfyUI caching: same seed reuses the cached result, new seed regenerates. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `api_key` | `STRING` | default `""` | Leave empty: the key is read from the C2C key store (GEMINI_API_KEY) or the GEMINI_API_KEY / GOOGLE_API_KEY environment variable. A key pasted here is moved into the key store and cleared, so it is never saved in the workflow or in image metadata. |
| `system_instruction` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | Optional system-level art direction applied to every generation (brand style, banned elements, palette rules). |
| `image_1` | `IMAGE` |  | Reference image(s). Batches flatten in order. |
| `image_2` | `IMAGE` |  | More reference image(s), after image_1. |
| `image_3` | `IMAGE` |  | More reference image(s), after image_2. |
| `image_4` | `IMAGE` |  | For style_transfer the LAST image is the style. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `images` | `IMAGE` | The generated images, one per batch_count call, resized to the first one's size. |
| 1 | `text_response` | `STRING` | The model's text reply (empty with response_modalities = IMAGE). |
| 2 | `info` | `STRING` | Model, mode, size, reference count, latency and tokens used (JSON). |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Audio


### AudioReverserMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Audio Reverser

Reverses the audio waveform (torch.flip on the sample dim), preserving sample rate and channels. TIMEMAP maps original to reversed timestamps (t_new = duration - t_orig). Optional: reverse only a time range, or reverse the grain order so notes keep their attacks.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/fluid_shots_audio.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `audio` | `AUDIO` |  | Standard ComfyUI audio ({'waveform': [B,C,S], 'sample_rate': int}). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `start_seconds` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…86400.0, step 0.01 | Reverse only from this time on (seconds); audio before it plays unchanged. 0 with end_seconds -1 = the whole clip (as before). |
| `end_seconds` | `FLOAT` | default `-1.0`, range -1.0…86400.0, step 0.01 | Reverse only up to this time (seconds); audio after it plays unchanged. -1 = the end of the clip. The clip keeps its length, and time_map lists the reversed and the untouched ranges as segments. |
| `reverse_mode` | choice: `reverse`, `grain_reverse (keeps note attacks)` | default `"reverse"` | reverse = every sample backwards (as before; notes swell instead of decaying). grain_reverse = the order of short grains runs backwards while each grain plays forwards, so notes keep their pitch and attack; only the order of events is reversed. |
| `grain_ms` | `FLOAT` | default `80.0`, range 10.0…1000.0, step 1.0 | grain_reverse only: grain length in milliseconds (50 % overlap). Shorter = finer time order, longer = smoother sound. A range shorter than two grains is reversed plainly. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `audio` | `AUDIO` | The audio played backwards (same sample rate and channels). |
| 1 | `time_map` | `TIMEMAP` | Maps original to reversed timestamps (t_new = duration - t_orig) for syncing video; with a time range it also lists each range as a segment. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Color Science


### C2CVAEQualityDecode

**Shown in the menu as:** VAE Decode (C2C)

VAE decode with optional fp32 (<=0.17 dB measured gain), spatial-only tiling for Wan video, optional unclamped output for HDR chains, optional ACES tone mapping, and an optional clean step that measures and corrects colour cast, oversaturation and decode speckle (also for an already-decoded image).

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/hdr_color_science.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `force_fp32` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Decode in fp32. Measured gain: at most 0.17 dB on Flux/Wan 2.1 round-trip (~2x VAE memory). |
| `tile_size` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1024, step 64 | Spatial tile size in PIXELS, used when tile_mode is manual (0 = no tiling). Converted with the VAE spatial factor (usually 8). 512 measured best for Wan on 8 GB. |
| `apply_aces` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Apply ACES filmic tone mapping after decode (the decode is treated as sRGB: linearised, tone-mapped with the ACES fit, encoded once). |
| `exposure` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.01…10.0, step 0.05 | Exposure for ACES (only used if apply_aces=True). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `clamp_output` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Core clamps decoder output to 0..1. Off keeps out-of-range values (4.6x / 5.3x more accurate there on Flux/Wan, measured M2). For HDR/EXR chains. |
| `tile_mode` | choice: `auto`, `off`, `manual` | default `"auto"` | auto: tile only when the untiled decode would not fit the GPU (measured on an 8 GB card: a 2K Wan decode took 502 s untiled, spilling into system RAM, and 37 s tiled, with the same picture); a tile_size above 0 is used as given. off: never tile. manual: tile at tile_size. Video is tiled in space only - splitting a video VAE in time costs ~21 dB. |
| `samples` | `LATENT` |  | The latent to decode (with vae). Leave empty to clean 'image'. |
| `vae` | `VAE` |  | The VAE that made the latent (needed with samples). |
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | An already-decoded picture to clean instead of decoding a latent. |
| `clean` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Measure the decode and correct what is wrong with it: colour cast, oversaturation, crushed shadows, chroma speckle. The report says what it found, including clipping it cannot undo. |
| `balance_mode` | choice: `off`, `grey world`, `match reference`, `white point` | default `"off"` | Clean (when clean is on): off = measure only. grey world = drive the AVERAGE neutral. Fast and wrong on any shot that is genuinely one colour - a sunset averages red and this will throw the sunset away. white point = drive the BRIGHTEST 1% neutral. Safer on coloured scenes, because a red room still has a neutral highlight. match reference = copy a plate's channel means. Use when you have the real footage. |
| `balance_strength` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | Clean (when clean is on): How far toward neutral. 1.0 is full correction; back it off when the cast is partly the lighting you wanted. |
| `saturation` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.01 | Clean (when clean is on): Chroma scale about luma, so pulling it down does not also darken the picture. 1.0 leaves it alone. If the render is oversaturated AND contrasty, read the report first - that combination is usually CFG, and lowering CFG keeps detail that desaturating here cannot bring back. |
| `contrast_restore` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | Clean (when clean is on): Lift crushed shadows, which brings contrast DOWN - the fix for a render that came back too hard. Raises the floor rather than stretching the range, so nothing can clip. It does NOT recover detail already crushed to black: those pixels just stop being pure black. The report says how much is in that state. |
| `chroma_cleanup` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | Clean (when clean is on): Smooth colour noise without touching detail. Blurs CHROMA only - the eye carries detail in luma, which is why every codec subsamples chroma and not luma, and why this can go quite hard before anything looks soft. The fix for VAE decode speckle. |
| `reference` | `IMAGE` |  | Clean (when clean is on): Required by 'match reference'. The plate, or any frame whose colour you want copied. |
| `restore_unchanged` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…0.5, step 0.005 | Clean (when clean is on): Experimental. 0 = off. Every VAE round trip loses detail everywhere, even where nothing was meant to change. With the original plate wired to 'reference', this writes the original pixels back wherever the picture did not really change, and keeps the edit. The value is how far the local average may move and still count as unchanged. Measured on the Flux VAE: one round trip moves it by up to 0.037, three by up to 0.050 - so start near 0.05-0.07. Too low leaves VAE loss in place; too high also reverts subtle intended changes. |
| `restore_radius` | `INT` | default `8`, range 1…64 | Clean (when clean is on): Window for the write-back, in pixels (8 = one latent cell of an 8x VAE). An edit's influence reaches about 3x this far past its edge, so the boundary blends rather than cuts. Experimental. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `IMAGE` | `IMAGE` | The decoded (and, with clean on, corrected) picture. |
| 1 | `report` | `STRING` | Clean: what was measured and corrected (empty when clean is off). |
| 2 | `cast_strength` | `FLOAT` | Clean: how strong the colour cast was. |
| 3 | `saturation` | `FLOAT` | Clean: measured saturation. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Control


### ControlAOVC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Control AOV — Multi-Control Fusion (C2C)

VFX-AOV control fusion: emit depth/canny/pose/normal/motion/ID as separate passes, a channel-packed image (depth=R/canny=G/pose=B), and a convenience blend. Runs Canny + optical-flow motion internally; accepts depth/pose/normal/ID maps as inputs. Feeds any ControlNet / union / control-video. Stack the separate passes for maximum spatial lock.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/control_forge.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `blend_mode` | choice: `screen`, `lighten_max`, `linear_dodge`, `multiply`, `average`, `weighted_avg`, `overlay` | default `"screen"` | How the 'blended' overlay combines passes. screen = least-destructive; linear_dodge clips; multiply darkens. |
| `preview_layout` | choice: `horizontal_3`, `vertical_3`, `grid_2x2` | default `"horizontal_3"` | Layout for the 'combined' output: horizontal_3 = depth \| pose \| canny; grid_2x2 = depth\|pose // canny\|original. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | Source frames — preprocessors below run on this. |
| `depth_model` | choice: `off`, `da_v2`, `da_v1`, `midas`, `da3`, `depth_pro`, `depthcrafter`, `dvd`, … (+4) | default `"off"` | Depth backend. VENDORED (run inside this pack): da_v2/da_v1/midas (transformers), da3 (Depth-Anything-3), depth_pro, depthcrafter (video), dvd (DVD — deterministic Wan-2.1 VIDEO depth, EnVision-Research; needs the DVD ckpt in models/DVD/, CC BY-NC). The depth_anything_* options delegate to comfyui_controlnet_aux. 'off' = wire an external depth map. NON-COMMERCIAL weights: dvd (CC BY-NC), depth_pro (apple/DepthPro-hf, Apple research licence); see depth_size for Depth-Anything. |
| `normal_model` | choice: `off`, `sobel_from_depth`, `normalcrafter` | default `"off"` | Normal backend (vendored): sobel_from_depth (no model) or normalcrafter (video). 'off' = wire an external normal map. |
| `depth_size` | choice: `small`, `medium`, `large`, `giant` | default `"small"` | DepthAnything backbone: small=ViT-S (fastest, test default), medium=ViT-B, large=ViT-L. giant: only DA3 has one; da_v2 / da_v1 / midas use large and the run note says so. Ignored by metric/zoe. Licences: Depth-Anything-V2 small = Apache-2.0; V2 base / large and DA3 large / giant (DA3NESTED-GIANT-LARGE) = CC BY-NC 4.0, NON-COMMERCIAL. |
| `depth_custom_ckpt` | choice: `(auto)`, `depth_anything_v2_vits.pth`, `depth_anything_v2_vitb.pth`, `depth_anything_v2_vitl.pth`, `depth_anything_v2_vitg.pth`, `depth_anything_vits14.pth`, `depth_anything_vitb14.pth`, `depth_anything_vitl14.pth` | default `"(auto)"` | Pick your own depth model, or '(auto)' = use the backend/size default. |
| `pose_model` | choice: `off`, `vitpose`, `dwpose`, `openpose`, `animal_pose`, `densepose` | default `"off"` | Pose backend. vitpose = WanV2 ViTPose chain (uses models/detection/*.onnx). dwpose/openpose/... delegate to comfyui_controlnet_aux. |
| `pose_ckpt` | `STRING` | default `""` | Custom ViTPose .onnx pose model filename (none auto-detected; type one or leave blank for default). |
| `id_matte_model` | choice: `off`, `sam_auto` | default `"off"` | ID/segmentation matte. sam_auto = automatic SAM segmentation (controlnet_aux SAMPreprocessor). 'off' = wire an external matte. |
| `edge_model` | choice: `internal_canny`, `off`, `canny`, `lineart`, `anyline`, `hed`, `pidinet`, `teed` | default `"internal_canny"` | internal_canny = OpenCV (no model). Others delegate to controlnet_aux. |
| `run_canny` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Used only when edge_model = internal_canny. |
| `canny_low` | `INT` | default `100`, range 0…255 | Canny lower threshold: weaker edges are kept only where they join a strong edge. Lower = more detail lines. |
| `canny_high` | `INT` | default `200`, range 0…255 | Canny upper threshold: gradients above it are always edges. Lower = more edges. |
| `canny_aperture` | choice: `3`, `5`, `7` | default `3` | Sobel aperture for internal Canny (odd 3/5/7). |
| `depth_invert` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Invert depth (1 - depth). Use when source is 'far=bright' but the ControlNet expects 'near=bright'. |
| `preproc_resolution` | `INT` | default `512`, range 64…4096, step 8 | Resolution passed to delegated preprocessors. |
| `run_motion` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Optical-flow motion-vector pass (needs an image batch ≥ 2 frames). |
| `depth_weight` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.05 | How much the depth pass counts in the blended output (0 = left out). The separate pass outputs are not scaled. |
| `canny_weight` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.05 | How much the canny pass counts in the blended output (0 = left out). The separate pass outputs are not scaled. |
| `pose_weight` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.05 | How much the pose pass counts in the blended output (0 = left out). The separate pass outputs are not scaled. |
| `normal_weight` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.05 | How much the normal pass counts in the blended output (0 = left out). The separate pass outputs are not scaled. |
| `match_to` | choice: `depth`, `canny`, `pose`, `image`, `largest` | default `"image"` | Internal: align every AOV pass to this source's size before resize/output. |
| `resize` | choice: `off`, `width/height`, `scale` | default `"off"` | Resize ALL outputs in-node. off = keep source size; width/height = resize to an exact width x height; scale = multiply size by 'scale'. |
| `width` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 0…16384, step 8 | Target width (resize=width/height). 0 = derive from height + aspect. |
| `height` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 0…16384, step 8 | Target height (resize=width/height). 0 = derive from width + aspect. |
| `scale` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.05…8.0, step 0.05 | Scale factor applied to source size (resize=scale). |
| `divisible_by` | `INT` | default `16`, range 1…512, step 1 | Snap output W/H to a multiple (VAE-safe: 16=Wan, 64=SD). 1 = no snapping. |
| `fit` | choice: `stretch`, `pad`, `crop` | default `"crop"` | Aspect handling: stretch = distort to fit; pad = letterbox with pad_color; crop = centre-crop to fill. |
| `resize_filter` | choice: `lanczos`, `bicubic`, `hamming`, `bilinear`, `box`, `nearest` | default `"lanczos"` | Resample filter. lanczos = sharpest; nearest = hard pixels. |
| `pad_color` | `STRING` | default `"#000000"` | Pad colour (hex), used only when fit=pad. |
| `depth` | `IMAGE` |  | Your own depth map (Depth Anything, DepthCrafter, ZoeDepth...). Wins over depth_model; depth_invert and depth_weight still apply. |
| `canny` | `IMAGE` |  | Your own edge map. Wins over edge_model. |
| `pose` | `IMAGE` |  | Your own pose render (DWPose / OpenPose / ViTPose). Wins over pose_model. |
| `normal` | `IMAGE` |  | Your own normal map (NormalCrafter...). Wins over normal_model. |
| `id_matte` | `IMAGE` |  | Your own ID / segmentation matte (SAM, cryptomatte). Wins over id_matte_model. |
| `allow_download` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Let the model backends download missing weights from Hugging Face (some are many GB). Off: only installed copies are used (a models folder or the Hugging Face cache) and the info output says what is missing. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `blended` | `IMAGE` | Single image, all passes OVERLAID via the chosen blend mode. |
| 1 | `combined` | `IMAGE` | Single image, depth \| pose \| canny shown SIDE-BY-SIDE (or grid) — the clearest 'all 3 at once' view. |
| 2 | `channel_packed` | `IMAGE` | depth=R, canny=G, pose=B — lossless packing for union ControlNets. |
| 3 | `depth` | `IMAGE` | Depth AOV (passthrough). |
| 4 | `canny` | `IMAGE` | Canny AOV (run internally if 'image' wired). |
| 5 | `pose` | `IMAGE` | Pose AOV (passthrough). |
| 6 | `normal` | `IMAGE` | Normal AOV (passthrough). |
| 7 | `motion` | `IMAGE` | Motion-vector AOV (optical flow, run internally on a frame batch). |
| 8 | `id_matte` | `IMAGE` | ID / segmentation matte AOV (passthrough). |
| 9 | `info` | `STRING` | Per-pass STATUS + recommended max-control wiring. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Diagnostics


### MaskFailureExplainerMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Mask Failure Explainer — Diagnostics

Diagnose why a mask might be failing. Analyzes brightness, blur, boundary contrast, color confusion, and background complexity. Outputs a detailed explanation, problem heatmap, severity score, and suggested masking method.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/mask_failure_explainer.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | Input image(s) — (B,H,W,C) float32 [0,1]. |
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Mask to diagnose — (B,H,W) float32 [0,1]. Can be from any segmentation method. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ring_width` | `INT` | default `5`, range 1…50, step 1 | Width in pixels of the boundary ring used for contrast/color analysis. |
| `blur_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `50.0`, range 0.0…1000.0, step 1.0 | Laplacian variance threshold below which the image is considered blurry. |
| `brightness_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.15`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Mean brightness threshold below which the scene is considered dark. |
| `area_jump_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.25`, range 0.01…10.0, step 0.01 | Timeline: a frame whose mask area changes by more than this fraction of the previous frame's area is flagged 'area_jump' (0.25 = 25 %). |
| `preview_fix` | choice: `none`, `auto`, `fill_holes`, `remove_specks`, `close_gaps`, `smooth_edges`, `dilate`, `erode`, … (+1) | default `"none"` | Corrective-action preview (non-destructive): apply one mask operation and return the result as corrected_mask with before/after mask metrics in fix_report. auto picks from the findings (holes -> fill_holes, fragments -> remove_specks, jagged -> smooth_edges, whole frame -> invert). none: corrected_mask is the analysed mask. |
| `fix_radius_px` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…64, step 1 | Radius in pixels for dilate / erode / close_gaps / smooth_edges. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `explanation` | `STRING` | Human-readable diagnosis explaining likely failure causes. |
| 1 | `problem_regions_mask` | `MASK` | Heatmap mask highlighting regions most likely to be problematic. |
| 2 | `severity_score` | `FLOAT` | Overall severity score in [0, 100] (higher means more issues). |
| 3 | `suggested_method` | `STRING` | Suggested masking method or refinement to try next. |
| 4 | `timeline_json` | `STRING` | Per-frame metrics and problem flags (dark, blurry, low boundary contrast, colour confusion, busy background, empty / whole-frame mask, area and centroid jumps) plus the list of problem frames, as JSON. |
| 5 | `corrected_mask` | `MASK` | The mask after the preview_fix operation (the analysed mask unchanged when preview_fix is none). |
| 6 | `fix_report` | `STRING` | Which fix was applied and why, with the mask metrics before and after, as JSON. |


---

### ModelMetadataExtractorMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Model Metadata Extractor

Inspect model file metadata WITHOUT unpickling or loading weights. Safe to run on untrusted .ckpt files. Reports tensor count, params, training metadata, and a quick fingerprint.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/model_metadata_extractor.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `file_path` | `STRING` | default `""` | Absolute path to a model file (.safetensors / .pt / .pth / .ckpt). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `compute_fingerprint` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Compute SHA256 over (size, first 1 MB, last 1 MB). Suitable cache key; far faster than full-file hashing. |
| `compare_path` | `STRING` | default `""` | Checkpoint comparison: absolute path of a second model file. Compares tensor names, shapes and dtypes (safetensors) or archive members (torch zip) without loading weights. Result in comparison_json. Empty = off. |
| `integrity_report` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Provenance and integrity audit: format by magic bytes vs extension, safetensors offset / size checks, and callables a .ckpt / .pt pickle would run on load (read from the opcodes, never executed). Result in audit_json. |
| `full_hash` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Also hash the WHOLE file (SHA-256, comparable with the hashes model sites publish). Reads every byte: seconds per GB. Result in audit_json. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `metadata_json` | `STRING` | Full metadata as JSON (header fields, training tags, format). |
| 1 | `model_kind` | `STRING` | Detected model kind, one of: stable_diffusion_checkpoint, vae, lora, text_encoder, safetensors_other (any other safetensors file, e.g. a diffusion model or ControlNet), unknown. |
| 2 | `total_params` | `INT` | Total parameter count summed across all tensors. |
| 3 | `fingerprint` | `STRING` | SHA256 fingerprint over (size, first 1 MB, last 1 MB) when enabled. |
| 4 | `lineage_json` | `STRING` | License + lineage information as JSON when present in metadata. |
| 5 | `comparison_json` | `STRING` | compare_path set: tensors only in A / only in B, shape and dtype mismatches, parameter delta, as JSON. Empty when off. |
| 6 | `audit_json` | `STRING` | integrity_report or full_hash on: format check, full SHA-256, structure warnings, suspicious pickle callables and a verdict (ok / warnings / suspicious), as JSON. Empty when off. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Distributed


### C2CGPUInventory

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C GPU Inventory

Lists the GPUs (architecture Turing to Blackwell, memory, BF16 / FP8 / FP4 support, whether this torch build has kernels for them), how they are linked (P2P, NVLink), Grace / aarch64 hosts, whether NCCL / gloo really work here, and which multi-GPU modes this box can run.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/c2c_distributed/nodes.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `probe_backends` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Really create a NCCL / gloo process group in a subprocess to see whether it works (a few seconds, cached until ComfyUI restarts). Off: report only what the hardware says. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `qualify` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Run the real qualification: one process per physical GPU, all-reduce / all-gather / reduce-scatter checked, latency and bandwidth, a hung rank detected within the timeout, the worker mechanism on every GPU. Tens of seconds. Result: MULTI_GPU_VERIFIED only for two or more distinct GPUs that passed; otherwise SINGLE_GPU_ONLY / FAILED. |
| `live_monitor` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Also read the live state: free / total memory per GPU, this process's allocated / reserved memory, utilisation (needs pynvml, else null), the models ComfyUI has loaded on each GPU and the ComfyUI queue (running / pending prompts). On: the node re-reads on every queue. Result in live_json. |
| `workload_vram_gb` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…4096.0, step 0.5 | Workload suitability: memory one GPU needs for the job, in GB (weights + activations). 0 = off. Each GPU is checked with 10% headroom against its total memory (and its free memory when live_monitor is on). Result in suitability. |
| `workload_precision` | choice: `any`, `fp32`, `fp16`, `bf16`, `fp8`, `fp4` | default `"any"` | Workload suitability: the precision the job runs in. bf16 needs cc >= 8.0, fp8 tensor cores cc >= 8.9, fp4 tensor cores cc >= 10.0; older GPUs are reported as emulated (slower). |
| `workload_gpus` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…64 | Workload suitability: how many suitable GPUs the job needs (e.g. 2 for a two-GPU split). |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `summary` | `STRING` | Readable summary: GPUs, links, working backends and usable multi-GPU modes. |
| 1 | `report_json` | `STRING` | The full inventory (and qualification result when run) as JSON. |
| 2 | `live_json` | `STRING` | live_monitor on: per-GPU free / used memory, utilisation, loaded models and the ComfyUI queue, as JSON. Empty when off. |
| 3 | `suitability` | `STRING` | workload_vram_gb > 0: per GPU whether the workload fits (total / free memory, precision support, kernels) and a verdict, as JSON. An estimate, not a measured run. Empty when off. |


---

### C2CSequenceParallel

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C Multi-GPU Split

Splits every model call over the GPUs of this machine. Any model: the call's batch items (cond and uncond, an image batch, Tile Split's tiles) go to different GPUs, with no exchange at all. Wan 2.1 / 2.2 and MiniMax H3 also split the tokens of one item (Ulysses all-to-all / Ring, exact). One FULL model copy per GPU, made by ComfyUI's own multi-GPU clones: weight memory is not pooled (this is not model sharding / FSDP). Works on Windows and Linux; with one GPU the model passes through unchanged. Put it last in the model chain (after LoRA / attention nodes). Replaces core's 'MultiGPU CFG Split' for this model.

Limits: speed-up on real multi-GPU hardware is not measured yet; ControlNet and self-attention masks are refused on the token-split path; other models are batch-split only. The 'plan' output lists the mode, devices, memory per GPU and the communication path before sampling starts.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/c2c_distributed/nodes.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `model` | `MODEL` |  | The diffusion model to split; put this node last in the model chain (after LoRA / attention nodes). |
| `gpus` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…64 | How many GPUs to use; 0 = all that ComfyUI sees. Each GPU holds a full copy of the model. |
| `strategy` | choice: `auto`, `ulysses`, `ring` | default `"auto"` | auto: Ulysses as far as the head count and fast links allow, Ring for the rest (USP). ulysses: prefer head-parallel all-to-all (best on NVLink). ring: K/V blocks passed around (best across slow links). |
| `min_tokens_per_gpu` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 1…1048576 | Calls with fewer tokens per GPU run on one GPU: below this the exchange costs more than it saves. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fast_link_gpus` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…64 | How many GPUs share fast links (NVLink domain). 0 = all of them. Ulysses stays inside this many GPUs. |
| `rank_backend` | choice: `threads`, `processes (Linux, NCCL)` | default `"threads"` | threads: every GPU is driven by a thread of this ComfyUI process (Windows and Linux, no NCCL; any model). processes: one worker process per GPU with its own model copy, talking through NCCL (Linux server GPUs; Wan and MiniMax H3 only). Verified on CPU test ranks only so far - not yet on real GPUs. |
| `memory_policy` | choice: `off`, `report`, `fit` | default `"off"` | Memory-aware GPU selection. off: as before. report: list each GPU's free memory against one model copy (weights + headroom) in the plan. fit: also use only the GPUs that have room (in core's device order: the model's GPU first, then the others; the count stops at the first GPU without room). |
| `memory_headroom_gb` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…256.0, step 0.25 | Memory kept free per GPU on top of the model weights (activations, latents), for memory_policy report / fit. |
| `preflight_workers` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Before splitting, run a small real matrix multiply on every GPU that would be used and compare it with the CPU. A failed GPU (no kernels, driver error, wrong result) is left out together with the GPUs after it; a failed model GPU stops with an error. Takes well under a second. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `model` | `MODEL` | The model with every call split over the GPUs (unchanged with one GPU). |
| 1 | `plan` | `STRING` | The chosen mode, devices, memory per GPU and communication path, as text. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Face


### FacepointsLandmarksC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Face Landmarks (Facepoints, 620 + 68/98)

Dense face landmarks for any model: 620 points on Google's GNM Head mesh, each with its own error estimate, plus the standard 68-point (iBUG) and 98-point (WFLW) sets and both pupils, from myned-ai/facepoints. Outputs OpenPose POSE_KEYPOINT (face = 68 + 2 pupils, pixels), a white-dot control image, an overlay, a face mask and JSON with every set. Video: the box follows the face's own points frame to frame. Weights are CC BY-NC 4.0 (non-commercial use only) and are downloaded only with allow_download, into models/facepoints.

Advanced controls (the node's collapsible sections):appearance_ (colours), strength_ (opacity, intensity, pose confidence), resolution_ (processing and output size; everything stays aligned), screening_ (face / point confidence, how unsure points are drawn, rejected faces in JSON), mask_ (grow, shrink, feather, softness, threshold, hull smoothing, preview), temporal_ (landmark smoothing, scene cuts, stable face order). The defaults give the original output.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/facepoints_landmarks.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | One image or a video (frames). Any size. |
| `face_finder` | choice: `yunet (finds faces)`, `whole image (already a face crop)` | default `"yunet (finds faces)"` | How the face box is found. yunet: OpenCV's face finder (MIT, 0.2 MB, downloaded with allow_download). whole image: the image is already a face crop. yolo: an installed Ultralytics face model (models/ultralytics/bbox). A wired face_mask overrides this. |
| `max_faces` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…16 | Faces per frame, left to right. |
| `passes` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…5 | Network passes per face. Each pass re-frames the box on the previous points (the model's own procedure; 3 = the released default). |
| `track_video` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Video: start each frame's box from the face's points in the previous frame (steadier, and the finder only looks for new faces). Off: every frame is found on its own. |
| `landmark_set` | choice: `68 + iris (OpenPose face)`, `98 (WFLW)`, `620 dense (GNM Head)` | default `"68 + iris (OpenPose face)"` | The points drawn into landmark_image. pose_keypoint is always OpenPose's 68 + 2 pupils. |
| `point_size` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…16 | Dot radius in pixels for landmark_image and overlay. |
| `allow_download` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Allow downloading myned-ai/facepoints (416 MB, CC BY-NC 4.0 = non-commercial use only) and YuNet (0.2 MB, MIT) into models/facepoints when they are missing. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `face_mask` | `MASK` |  | A mask over the face (per frame, or one for all): its box is used instead of the face finder. One face. |
| `finder_confidence` | `FLOAT` | default `0.6`, range 0.05…0.99, step 0.01 | Minimum score for yunet / yolo faces. |
| `confident_pct` | `FLOAT` | default `2.5`, range 0.1…10.0, step 0.1 | A point counts as sure when its calibrated error estimate is below this % of the face size (2.5 = a little above a typical good point); its confidence c is 0.5 exactly there. Unsure points are drawn faint in overlay. |
| `appearance_scheme` | choice: `model card (default)`, `custom` | default `"model card (default)"` | Appearance. model card: the original colours (white dots on black; 620 points coloured by kind). custom: the colours below. Colours change the images only, never the POSE_KEYPOINT numbers. |
| `appearance_landmark_colour` | `STRING` | default `"#FFFFFF"` | Appearance (custom): dot colour in landmark_image, #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_background_colour` | `STRING` | default `"#000000"` | Appearance (custom): landmark_image background, #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_overlay_colour` | `STRING` | default `""` | Appearance (custom): one colour for every dot in overlay, #RRGGBB. Empty = the per-set / per-kind colours below. |
| `appearance_colour_lmk` | `STRING` | default `"#1E88E5"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'lmk', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_skin` | `STRING` | default `"#FF8C00"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'skin', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_lid` | `STRING` | default `"#00C850"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'lid', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_teeth_u` | `STRING` | default `"#E63296"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'teeth_u', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_teeth_l` | `STRING` | default `"#FF4DA6"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'teeth_l', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_iris` | `STRING` | default `"#FFD700"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 620-point kind 'iris', #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_68` | `STRING` | default `"#E63C3C"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 68 + iris set, #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_colour_98` | `STRING` | default `"#E63C3C"` | Appearance (custom): overlay colour of the 98 set, #RRGGBB. |
| `appearance_kinds_on_landmark_image` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Appearance (custom): landmark_image uses the overlay's per-set / per-kind colours instead of one dot colour. |
| `strength_overlay_opacity` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Strength: how opaque the overlay's dots are over the image (real alpha blending). 1 = solid. |
| `strength_dot_intensity` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Strength: landmark_image dot brightness, blended toward the background (a weaker control image). 1 = full colour. |
| `strength_low_confidence_opacity` | `FLOAT` | default `0.45`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Strength: how strong an unsure point is drawn - its colour factor in 'dim' mode, its opacity in 'fade' mode. |
| `strength_render_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Strength: a point whose confidence is below this is drawn as unsure (see screening_low_confidence_points). Drawing only. |
| `strength_pose_confidence_scale` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…4.0, step 0.01 | Strength: multiplies the confidence values in pose_keypoint (clamped to 0..1). Coordinates are unchanged. |
| `resolution_processing` | choice: `source`, `512`, `768`, `1024`, `1536`, `2048`, `custom` | default `"source"` | Resolution: size the faces are FOUND and cropped at. source = the input; a number = the longer side at most that (downscale only, aspect kept). The network itself always sees the checkpoint's own input size. Points come back in source pixels. |
| `resolution_processing_max` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 64…16384, step 8 | Resolution: the longer side for resolution_processing = custom. |
| `resolution_output` | choice: `original`, `512 x 512`, `768 x 768`, `1024 x 1024`, `1280 x 720`, `720 x 1280`, `1920 x 1080`, `1080 x 1920`, … (+1) | default `"original"` | Resolution: size of landmark_image, overlay, face_mask - and the coordinates in pose_keypoint and JSON follow it, so everything stays aligned. original = the input size. |
| `resolution_output_width` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 16…16384, step 8 | Resolution: width for resolution_output = custom. |
| `resolution_output_height` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 16…16384, step 8 | Resolution: height for resolution_output = custom. |
| `resolution_keep_aspect` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Resolution: fit inside the output size keeping the aspect ratio (the output is the fitted size). Off: stretch to exactly that size. |
| `screening_face_confidence_min` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Screening: faces whose overall landmark confidence is below this are left out of the images and pose_keypoint (the face finder's own threshold is finder_confidence). |
| `screening_point_confidence_min` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Screening: pose_keypoint points below this confidence get c = 0 (consumers skip them); their x, y stay. JSON always keeps every point and lists the screened ones. |
| `screening_low_confidence_points` | choice: `dim (default)`, `fade`, `hide`, `keep` | default `"dim (default)"` | Screening: how unsure 620-set points (below strength_render_threshold) are DRAWN - dim (the original look, overlay only), fade (transparent), hide, or keep (like sure points). fade / hide apply to landmark_image too. Data is never removed by this. |
| `screening_keep_rejected_faces` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Screening: list rejected faces (no face in the box, below the face confidence) in the JSON with the reason, for diagnosis. They never enter the images or pose. |
| `mask_grow` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…512 | Face mask: expand (dilate) by this many px. |
| `mask_shrink` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…512 | Face mask: contract (erode) by this many px (after mask_grow). |
| `mask_feather` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…256.0, step 0.5 | Face mask: Gaussian blur, sigma in px (soft on both sides of the edge). |
| `mask_edge_softness` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…256.0, step 0.5 | Face mask: fade in from the edge over this many px (the outer edge stays put). |
| `mask_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Face mask: > 0 makes the mask hard at this level (applied last). 0 = off. |
| `mask_hull_smoothing` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…6 | Face mask: rounds the hull outline (corner-cutting passes). 0 = the original hull. |
| `mask_preview` | choice: `off`, `tint overlay`, `outline on overlay` | default `"off"` | Face mask: show the mask on the overlay image (tint or outline). The MASK output is not changed by this. |
| `temporal_smoothing` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Temporal: smooth landmark trajectories over a video (adaptive: still points steady, real motion followed). Independent of track_video. Raw points stay in the JSON. |
| `temporal_strength` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…0.99, step 0.01 | Temporal: smoothing of still points. 0 = none; higher = steadier. A still point is averaged over about 1 / (1 - strength) frames (0.5: about 2, 0.8: about 5, 0.95: about 20); a point that moves faster than about (1 + 5 x strength) % of the face per frame follows at once. |
| `temporal_confidence_aware` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Temporal: points the model is less sure of than before move less toward their new position. |
| `temporal_max_offset` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…512.0, step 0.5 | Temporal: the most (px) smoothing may hold a point away from its detected position in a frame. 0 = no limit. |
| `temporal_scene_cut` | `FLOAT` | default `0.25`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Temporal: a frame that differs from the previous one by more than this (mean absolute difference, 0..1) is a scene cut: smoothing and identities restart. 0 = off. |
| `temporal_reset_on_loss` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Temporal: when a face is not found in a frame, its smoothing restarts when it comes back. |
| `temporal_stable_order` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Temporal: keep each face's position in pose_keypoint / JSON by identity across frames (matched by position). Off: left to right in every frame (the original order). |
| `strength_connection_lines` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Strength: draw the face contours (jaw, brows, nose, eyes, lips) as lines between the points - 0 = off. For the 68 and 98 sets (the 620 dense points have no contour order). Drawing only. |
| `appearance_line_width` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…16 | Appearance: connection line width in px (output pixels). |
| `mask_faces` | choice: `all faces`, `largest face`, `face index` | default `"all faces"` | Face mask: which faces fill face_mask - all, the largest, or one by its position in pose_keypoint. |
| `mask_face_index` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…15 | Face mask: the face for mask_faces = face index (0 = first in pose_keypoint). |
| `mask_part` | choice: `whole face (default)`, `eyes`, `eye (image left)`, `eye (image right)`, `eyelids`, `brows`, `nose`, `lips`, … (+9) | default `"whole face (default)"` | Face mask: which part of the head face_mask covers. Features (eyes, lids, brows, nose, lips, inner mouth) and areas (around the mouth, cheeks, chin + jaw) come from the landmarks; forehead, ears, hair and head + hair are ESTIMATED (no landmark covers them) and fitted to the picture by mask_head_refine. custom list: several parts joined, typed in mask_parts_custom. |
| `mask_parts_custom` | `STRING` | default `""` | Face mask (mask_part = custom list): parts joined into one mask, comma separated, e.g. 'eyes, lips' or 'head + hair (estimated), whole face'. |
| `mask_invert` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Face mask: everything EXCEPT the chosen parts (applied after grow / shrink / feather). |
| `mask_head_refine` | choice: `image edges (GrabCut)`, `off` | default `"image edges (GrabCut)"` | Face mask (estimated parts): image edges = fit the estimated forehead / ears / hair / head to the picture (GrabCut on a small crop, cheap); off = the geometric estimate only. |
| `mask_extend_up` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -100.0…300.0, step 1.0 | Face mask: extend the mask up by this % of the face height (chin to brows); negative shrinks that side. 'up' is the head's up when mask_extend_follow_tilt is on. |
| `mask_extend_down` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -100.0…300.0, step 1.0 | Face mask: extend the mask down by this % of the face height (chin to brows); negative shrinks that side. 'up' is the head's up when mask_extend_follow_tilt is on. |
| `mask_extend_left` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -100.0…300.0, step 1.0 | Face mask: extend the mask left by this % of the face height (chin to brows); negative shrinks that side. 'up' is the head's up when mask_extend_follow_tilt is on. |
| `mask_extend_right` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -100.0…300.0, step 1.0 | Face mask: extend the mask right by this % of the face height (chin to brows); negative shrinks that side. 'up' is the head's up when mask_extend_follow_tilt is on. |
| `mask_extend_follow_tilt` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Face mask: extend along the head's own axes (a tilted head is extended along its tilt); off = the picture's axes. |
| `mask_extend_falloff` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…300.0, step 1.0 | Face mask: soft fall-off of the extended band, % of the face height (0 = hard). |
| `screening_robust_detection` | choice: `auto (when a frame finds no face)`, `off`, `always` | default `"auto (when a frame finds no face)"` | Screening: low light, colour casts and motion blur. auto: a frame where no face is found is analysed and retried on enhanced copies (light, colour, deblur, a 1.5x look) and then with a nearby frame's face box; always: every frame is detected on the enhanced copy; off: as before. The copies are only shown to the detector - every output keeps the original footage. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `pose_keypoint` | `POSE_KEYPOINT` | OpenPose JSON per frame; face_keypoints_2d = 68 iBUG points + 2 pupils (70 x [x, y, c], pixels of the output size). strength_pose_confidence_scale / screening_point_confidence_min change c only. |
| 1 | `landmark_image` | `IMAGE` | The chosen landmark set as dots (white on black by default) - a control image. |
| 2 | `overlay` | `IMAGE` | The input with the points drawn (620: coloured by kind, unsure points dim / faded / hidden). |
| 3 | `face_mask` | `MASK` | Filled hull of the face points, 0..1 (grow / shrink / feather / softness / threshold when set). |
| 4 | `landmarks_json` | `STRING` | Every set per frame and face: 620 points with std, 68, 98, iris, box, confidence; raw points when smoothed; screened points and rejected faces when asked. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Frequency


### FilmGrainMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Film Grain (MEC)

Lightroom-style film grain with per-frame animation for video batches.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/frequency_grain/nodes.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-NKD-Basic-Tools by Nekodificador (MIT)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | Lightroom / Camera Raw-style film grain: Amount, Size, Roughness. Monochrome by default; raise Color for dye-cloud colour grain. |
| `amount` | `FLOAT` | default `25.0`, range 0.0…100.0, step 0.5 | How much grain shows through (Lightroom Amount). |
| `size` | `FLOAT` | default `25.0`, range 0.0…100.0, step 0.5 | Grain size — bigger = coarser; noise is generated at reduced resolution then upscaled. |
| `roughness` | `FLOAT` | default `50.0`, range 0.0…100.0, step 0.5 | Irregularity of the grain: blends a fine and a coarse field, like Lightroom. |
| `color` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…100.0, step 1.0 | 0 = clean monochrome grain. Raise for coloured dye-cloud grain. |
| `animate` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Fresh grain on every frame of the batch — real film shimmer. Off = the same static grain on all frames. |
| `seed` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…18446744073709551615 | Same seed, same grain. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Optional — confine the grain to the mask, feathered by its values (soft edges blend in). |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `image` | `IMAGE` | The image with grain added (inside the mask only when one is wired). |
| 1 | `report` | `STRING` | Settings used, the device, and any fallback (e.g. computed on the CPU after a GPU out-of-memory). |


---

### FrequencyCombineMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Frequency Combine (MEC)

Merge a high-frequency detail layer onto a low-frequency base. Output is clamped to 0..1 on the IMAGE socket — values above 1.0 are clipped; this is an 8-bit-range IMAGE, not a scene-linear plate.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/frequency_grain/nodes.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-NKD-Basic-Tools by Nekodificador (MIT)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `high_frequency` | `IMAGE` |  | Detail layer from Frequency Separate (mode/linear must match). |
| `low_frequency` | `IMAGE` |  | Base image the detail lands on (e.g. the relit result). Leaves Separate in display space when linear=True; Combine re-linearises it. |
| `mode` | choice: `Divide`, `Subtract` | default `"Divide"` | Must match the Separate that made the HF. |
| `linear` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Process in linear light (correct). Turn off to work in gamma like classic Photoshop. Must match between Separate and Combine. |
| `detail_strength` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…2.0, step 0.01 | Attenuate (<1) or boost (>1) the transferred detail. 0.0 restores the base only. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Optional — apply the detail only inside the mask (feathered by its values). |
| `mask_feather` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…64.0, step 1.0 | Soften the mask edge before applying detail. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `image` | `IMAGE` | The base with the detail layer merged back on. |
| 1 | `report` | `STRING` | What ran and any notes, as text. |


---

### FrequencySeparateMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Frequency Separate (MEC)

Split an image into low-frequency base and high-frequency detail. HF leaves in working space; LF leaves in display space when linear=True.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/frequency_grain/nodes.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-NKD-Basic-Tools by Nekodificador (MIT)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | Split into a soft base (low frequency) and a fine detail layer (high frequency). Pair with Frequency Combine to transfer texture. |
| `method` | choice: `Gaussian`, `Guided`, `Rolling Guidance`, `Median` | default `"Guided"` | How the base is smoothed. Guided keeps edges clean without the halo Gaussian leaves; Rolling Guidance erases texture by size while keeping shapes; Median is for spot blemishes (radius capped internally). |
| `radius` | `INT` | default `8`, range 1…128, step 1 | Detail scale. Larger = coarser base, more goes into the detail layer. Median mode caps the effective radius at 7 — see the report for what ran. |
| `edge_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.1`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | How strongly edges are protected (Guided / Rolling Guidance). Lower = sharper edges kept. |
| `mode` | choice: `Divide`, `Subtract` | default `"Divide"` | How detail is encoded. Divide (ratio) is lighting-invariant — best for transferring detail between differently-lit images. Negative ratios are discarded on Combine before recombine. |
| `detail` | choice: `Luminance`, `RGB` | default `"Luminance"` | Luminance keeps detail achromatic so a Divide recombine never touches color. RGB carries chromatic detail too. |
| `linear` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Process in linear light (correct). Turn off to work in gamma like classic Photoshop. Must match between Separate and Combine. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Optional — attenuate the detail layer outside the mask on high_frequency_masked. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `high_frequency` | `IMAGE` | The fine detail layer (no detail = 1.0 in Divide mode, 0.0 in Subtract). Wire to Frequency Combine. |
| 1 | `low_frequency` | `IMAGE` | The soft base layer with the detail removed. |
| 2 | `high_frequency_masked` | `IMAGE` | The detail layer kept only inside the mask (neutral elsewhere); same as high_frequency without a mask. |
| 3 | `report` | `STRING` | What ran: method, radius, mode and any notes, as text. |


---

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Helpers


### AspectPresetMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Size (C2C)

Width and height for a render: a common aspect ratio scaled to a long edge (Wan 480p / 720p presets give Wan's native sizes), or your own width x height - snapped to a multiple of N, because Wan, Flux and SDXL need sizes divisible by 8 / 16 / 64.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `preset` | choice: `1:1 square`, `16:9 landscape`, `9:16 portrait`, `4:3 landscape`, `3:4 portrait`, `21:9 ultrawide`, `2.39:1 cinema`, `Wan 480p land`, … (+4) | default `"16:9 landscape"` | An aspect ratio scaled to 'base', a Wan native size, or Custom (width x height below). |
| `base` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 64…8192 | Long edge target (ignored for Wan presets and Custom). |
| `multiple` | `INT` | default `64`, range 1…256 | Snap width and height to this multiple. Wan presets ignore it: their native sizes (832x480, 1280x720) are already what Wan needs (multiples of 16). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `width` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 8…16384 | Custom: width before snapping. |
| `height` | `INT` | default `1024`, range 8…16384 | Custom: height before snapping. |
| `direction` | choice: `down`, `nearest`, `up` | default `"down"` | Snap to the multiple below, the nearest one, or the one above. |
| `model_profile` | choice: `none (use multiple)`, `SD 1.5 / SDXL (x64)`, `SD3 / Flux / Qwen Image (x16)`, `Wan 2.1 / 2.2, HunyuanVideo (x16)`, `LTX-Video (x32)` | default `"none (use multiple)"` | Snap to what a model family needs instead of 'multiple': SD 1.5 / SDXL x64, SD3 / Flux / Qwen Image x16, Wan / HunyuanVideo x16, LTX-Video x32 (VAE downscale x patch size). Wan presets are already Wan-native and stay as they are. |
| `custom_profiles` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | Your own named sizes, one per line: 'Name = 1920x1080' (exact size, then snapped) or 'Name = 2.39:1' (ratio scaled to 'base'). Copy this text between workflows to share a project's profiles. '#' starts a comment. |
| `profile` | `STRING` | default `""` | Name of a line in custom_profiles to use instead of 'preset'. Empty: use 'preset'. An unknown name stops the run and lists the names. |
| `overscan_pct` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…50.0, step 0.5 | Render this many percent larger than the picked size (overscan margin to crop back later), then snap. 0 = off. Also applies to Wan presets (then snapped to x16 or the model profile). |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `width` | `INT` | Width in pixels, snapped to the multiple. |
| 1 | `height` | `INT` | Height in pixels, snapped to the multiple. |


---

### ConditionalSwitchMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Conditional Switch

Return value_true when condition is True, else value_false. Both inputs are wildcard (*) so it works for any type. Optional: decide by a typed comparison (compare_a <operator> compare_b), or route one of up to five branches by index, with an explicit fallback.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `condition` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | On: pass value_true. Off: pass value_false. |
| `value_true` | `*` |  | Passed on when condition is on (only this branch runs). |
| `value_false` | `*` |  | Passed on when condition is off (only this branch runs). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `source` | choice: `condition`, `compare`, `index` | default `"condition"` | What picks the branch. condition: the switch above. compare: compare_a <operator> compare_b (true -> value_true, false -> value_false). index: the branch number below (0 value_false, 1 value_true, 2-4 value_2..value_4). |
| `index` | `INT` | default `1`, range -1…99 | source 'index': 0 = value_false, 1 = value_true, 2..4 = value_2..value_4. A number without a wired branch uses 'fallback' (an error when that is not wired either). |
| `compare_a` | `*` |  | source 'compare': the value to test (any type: number, text, boolean, a tensor for 'is empty'). |
| `operator` | choice: `==`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `contains`, `starts with`, … (+4) | default `"=="` | source 'compare': how compare_a is tested against compare_b. contains / starts with / ends with / matches regex compare as text; 'is empty' / 'is not empty' ignore compare_b. |
| `compare_b` | `STRING` | default `""` | source 'compare': the value compare_a is tested against, typed as compare_as says. |
| `compare_as` | choice: `auto`, `number`, `text`, `boolean` | default `"auto"` | source 'compare': read both sides as numbers, text or booleans (true/false, yes/no, on/off, 1/0). auto: from compare_a's type. A side that cannot be read that way stops the run with a message. |
| `value_2` | `*` |  | source 'index': branch 2 (only the chosen branch runs). |
| `value_3` | `*` |  | source 'index': branch 3 (only the chosen branch runs). |
| `value_4` | `*` |  | source 'index': branch 4 (only the chosen branch runs). |
| `fallback` | `*` |  | Used when the chosen branch is not wired or the index is out of range. Not wired: that case stops the run with a message instead. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `out` | `*` | value_true or value_false, whichever the condition chose. |


---

### ExecutionTimerMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Execution Timer (C2C)

Stopwatch: returns seconds since the *previous* execution of the same label. First tick returns 0. Passes input through unchanged.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `passthrough` | `*` |  | Anything: passed on unchanged; the timer ticks when it arrives. |
| `label` | `STRING` | default `"stage_a"` | Timers with the same label measure from each other's ticks. |
| `reset` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | If true, this tick is treated as the first. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `since_label` | `STRING` | default `""` | Measure from the last tick of THIS other label instead of this label's own last tick: put a timer 'start' before a stage and one with since_label 'start' after it to time exactly that stage (stage name 'start->label'). Empty: as before. |
| `history_size` | `INT` | default `50`, range 1…10000 | How many measurements of this stage are kept (newest) for median / p95 in stats_json. Kept in memory until ComfyUI restarts. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `passthrough` | `*` | The input, unchanged. |
| 1 | `report` | `STRING` | '[label] +seconds' for this tick. |
| 2 | `elapsed_seconds` | `FLOAT` | Seconds since the previous tick with the same label (0 on the first). |
| 3 | `stats_json` | `STRING` | JSON: this stage's kept measurements (count, last, median, p95, min, max, total), RAM / VRAM in use now, and a tree of every stage grouped by '/' in the labels (e.g. 'render/decode' adds to 'render'). First ticks and resets are not measurements and are not kept. |


---

### ImageBatchSliceMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Batch Range (C2C)

Pick frames from an IMAGE batch: a start:end:step range (negative values count from the end, step=2 keeps every second frame), a split at a frame index or a fraction, a single frame (first, middle, last or by index), a list of ranges in frames, seconds or timecodes, or the batch re-sampled to another frame rate.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `images` | `IMAGE` |  | The frames to pick from (an IMAGE batch). |
| `start` | `INT` | default `0`, range -100000…100000 | Range mode: first frame. Negative counts from the end. |
| `end` | `INT` | default `-1`, range -100000…100000 | Range mode: exclusive end. -1 = end of batch. |
| `step` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…1024 | Range mode: keep every Nth frame. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | choice: `range`, `split at index`, `split at ratio`, `first frame`, `middle frame`, `last frame`, `frame at index`, `ranges`, … (+1) | default `"range"` | What to pick: a start:end:step range, a split (at a frame index or a fraction), or one frame (first, middle, last or by index). |
| `split_index` | `INT` | default `1`, range 0…100000 | 'split at index': frames before this index go to 'images'. |
| `split_ratio` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | 'split at ratio': this fraction of the batch goes to 'images'. |
| `frame_index` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…999999 | 'frame at index': 0-based, clamped to the last frame. |
| `ranges` | `STRING` | default `"0..9"` | 'ranges' mode: comma-separated ranges, ends included, e.g. '0..9, 20..29/2, -5..'. '/N' keeps every Nth frame, a negative number counts from the end, an open end runs to the last frame. Positions may be seconds ('1.5s..3s') or timecodes ('00:00:02:00..00:00:03:12', HH:MM:SS:FF) when fps is set. 'images' gets the frames in the order listed, 'remainder' every frame not picked. |
| `fps` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1000.0, step 0.001 | Frame rate of the incoming batch. 'ranges': needed only for seconds / timecodes. 'resample to fps': the source rate. |
| `target_fps` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1000.0, step 0.001 | 'resample to fps': the new frame rate. Each output frame is the source frame nearest its time, so the clip keeps its duration (e.g. 30 -> 24 drops frames, 24 -> 48 repeats them). |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `images` | `IMAGE` | The selected frames (the first part when splitting). |
| 1 | `frame_count` | `INT` | How many frames 'images' holds. |
| 2 | `remainder` | `IMAGE` | Split modes: the frames after the split point. Empty in the other modes. |
| 3 | `remainder_count` | `INT` | How many frames 'remainder' holds. |
| 4 | `total_frames` | `INT` | How many frames came in. |
| 5 | `is_video` | `BOOLEAN` | True when more than one frame came in. |


---

### ImageStatsProbeMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Probe (C2C)

Pass-through probe for debugging: wire an image, a mask and/or a latent and read their statistics - black or blown-out frames, how much of each frame a mask covers, NaN / Inf or saturated latents.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `images` | `IMAGE` |  | An image to measure (passed on unchanged). |
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | A mask to measure coverage of (passed on unchanged). |
| `latent` | `LATENT` |  | ComfyUI LATENT dict (must contain 'samples'). |
| `threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Mask: a pixel counts as covered above this value. |
| `fail_on_corrupt` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Latent: stop the run when it holds NaN or Inf. |
| `scope` | choice: `none`, `histogram`, `waveform`, `rgb parade` | default `"none"` | Draw a scope of the image batch on the 'scope' output: histogram (R, G, B), waveform (luma per column) or rgb parade. |
| `temporal_check` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Measure every frame and flag sudden exposure, colour, clipping or mask (alpha) jumps and frames with NaN / Inf (frame_stats_json, anomaly_count). |
| `anomaly_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.05`, range 0.001…1.0, step 0.001 | temporal_check: a frame is flagged when luma, a channel mean or the mask mean moves more than this (0..1 scale) from the previous frame, or the clipped share moves more than this fraction. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `images` | `IMAGE` | The image, unchanged. |
| 1 | `report` | `STRING` | One line per wired input (image stats, mask coverage, latent verdict). |
| 2 | `mean` | `FLOAT` | Image mean (0 when no image is wired). |
| 3 | `std` | `FLOAT` | Image standard deviation. |
| 4 | `bright_pct` | `FLOAT` | Percent of image pixels brighter than 0.95. |
| 5 | `mask` | `MASK` | The mask, unchanged. |
| 6 | `coverage_mean_pct` | `FLOAT` | Mean per-frame mask coverage in percent (pixels above threshold). |
| 7 | `coverage_min_pct` | `FLOAT` | Lowest per-frame coverage in percent. |
| 8 | `coverage_max_pct` | `FLOAT` | Highest per-frame coverage in percent. |
| 9 | `latent` | `LATENT` | The latent, unchanged. |
| 10 | `info_json` | `STRING` | Latent shape, dtype, per-channel stats, NaN / Inf counts and verdict as JSON. |
| 11 | `verdict` | `STRING` | Latent verdict: healthy / low_contrast / saturated / corrupt. |
| 12 | `nan_count` | `INT` | NaN elements in the latent. |
| 13 | `inf_count` | `INT` | Inf elements in the latent. |
| 14 | `scope` | `IMAGE` | The chosen scope of the whole image batch (histogram, luma waveform or RGB parade; 256 px high, log density; long clips and large frames are sampled). A black 64x64 picture when scope is 'none'. |
| 15 | `frame_stats_json` | `STRING` | temporal_check on: JSON with per-frame stats (luma, channel means, clipped %, NaN, Inf, mask mean) and the flagged frames with the reason. '' when off. |
| 16 | `anomaly_count` | `INT` | temporal_check on: how many frames were flagged (0 when off). |
| 17 | `width` | `INT` | Width of the image (or mask) in pixels; 0 when only a latent is wired. |
| 18 | `height` | `INT` | Height of the image (or mask) in pixels; 0 when only a latent is wired. |
| 19 | `frame_count` | `INT` | Frames in the image batch (else the mask, else the latent batch). |

### NumberLerpMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Number Lerp (C2C)

Linear / smoothstep / cosine interpolation between two values. t is clamped to [0,1].

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/helpers/helpers.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `a` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -1000000000.0…1000000000.0, step 0.0001 | The value at t = 0. |
| `b` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range -1000000000.0…1000000000.0, step 0.0001 | The value at t = 1. |
| `t` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.001 | How far from a to b (0..1). |
| `curve` | choice: `linear`, `smoothstep`, `cosine`, `hold` | default `"linear"` | linear: straight; smoothstep and cosine ease in and out near a and b; hold: keeps the earlier value until the next key (steps). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `keyframes` | `STRING` | default `""` | Optional keyframes 'position:value', comma separated, e.g. '0:0, 0.5:1, 1:0' (positions in the time_mode's unit: 0..1, frames or seconds). When set, a and b are ignored and curve shapes each segment; before the first / after the last key the value holds. |
| `time_mode` | choice: `normalised t`, `frame`, `seconds` | default `"normalised t"` | normalised t: evaluate at t (0..1) as before. frame / seconds: evaluate at 'time' instead; without keyframes t = time / duration. |
| `time` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -1000000000.0…1000000000.0, step 0.001 | frame / seconds mode: where to evaluate (a frame number or seconds). |
| `duration` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1000000000.0, step 0.001 | frame / seconds mode: length of one cycle in the same unit. Needed without keyframes; with keyframes 0 = up to the last key. |
| `loop` | choice: `clamp`, `loop`, `ping-pong` | default `"clamp"` | frame / seconds mode: clamp holds the end value, loop restarts every cycle, ping-pong runs forward then back. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `float` | `FLOAT` | The interpolated value. |
| 1 | `int` | `INT` | The interpolated value, rounded to the nearest whole number. |
