### FolderVersionControl

**Shown in the menu as:** Folder Version Control (check / set)

Check or set the version counter for a label/date folder. 'check' reports how many vNNN folders exist and what the next one will be; 'set' creates placeholders up to a number so the next run continues after it. To truly reset a label, delete its date folder from disk.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/folder_incrementer.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `action` | choice: `check`, `set` | default `"check"` | check -> report how many versions exist and what comes next. Reads only; touches nothing. set -> create empty placeholder directories up to `value`, so the next run produces value+1. Use it to skip ahead or to reserve a range. |
| `label` | `STRING` | default `"default"` | Folder name to inspect, under the output directory. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `value` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…999999 | 'set' only: create placeholders up to this version number. Ignored when checking. |
| `trigger` | `*` |  | Optional any-type trigger. Wire any upstream output here to control when this runs. |
| `prefix` | `STRING` | default `"v"` | Version prefix. Must match the incrementer's. |
| `padding` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…10 | Zero-pad width. Must match the incrementer's. |
| `base_path` | `STRING` | default `""` | Override base directory. Empty -> ComfyUI output dir. |
| `date_format` | choice: `MM-DD-YYYY`, `DD-MM-YYYY`, `YYYY-MM-DD` | default `"MM-DD-YYYY"` | Must match the incrementer's. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `status` | `STRING` | Human-readable description of what was found or done. |
| 1 | `current_version` | `INT` | Highest version that exists (0 when there are none yet). |
| 2 | `next_version` | `INT` | What the next incrementer run will produce. |


---

### FolderVersionVariant

**Shown in the menu as:** Folder Version Variant (per output)

Give one output its own prefix or suffix while keeping the version the Folder Version Incrementer decided. Put one of these between the incrementer and each save node when a single render writes several outputs - a mov, an exr sequence, a png sequence, a masked mov - so they all file under one version with different names.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/folder_incrementer.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `filename_prefix` | `STRING` | default `""` | Connect the Folder Version Incrementer's `filename_prefix` output. Every variant fed from the same incrementer lands under the same version. |
| `suffix` | `STRING` | default `""` | Tag for THIS output, e.g. '_masked', '_exr', '_png'. Leave empty for the one output that needs no tag. |
| `mode` | choice: `filename`, `subfolder`, `folder` | default `"subfolder"` | Where the tag goes. filename -> shot_a/v004/shot_a_masked.mov everything in one folder, told apart by name subfolder -> shot_a/v004/masked/shot_a.mov one folder per output INSIDE the version. Use this for exr sequences - thousands of files should not share a directory with anything else. folder -> shot_a_masked/v004/shot_a.mov a separate top-level tree, still on the same version. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prefix` | `STRING` | default `""` | Tag placed BEFORE the name instead of after. Offered because a prefix faked with a suffix sorts in the wrong order, which is the whole reason to use one. |
| `version_string` | `STRING` | default `""` | Optional. Connect the incrementer's `version_string` so a subfolder tag is placed immediately after the version directory rather than at the end of the path. |
| `extension` | `STRING` | default `""` | Optional extension for the `output_filename` output, e.g. '.mov'. Most save nodes take `filename_prefix` and add their own. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `filename_prefix` | `STRING` | Re-tagged prefix. Wire to the save node's filename_prefix. |
| 1 | `subfolder_path` | `STRING` | Directory part only. |
| 2 | `output_filename` | `STRING` | Full name including the extension, when one was given. |
| 3 | `report` | `STRING` | What was tagged and where, and what stayed shared. |


---

### ParameterHistoryMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Parameter History

Query the parameter history database. Shows what parameters were changed, when, and what the previous values were. Supports run-to-run diffs.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/parameter_memory.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | choice: `all_history`, `last_run_diff`, `node_class_filter` | default `"all_history"` | all_history: show recent parameter changes across all nodes last_run_diff: show what changed between the last two runs node_class_filter: filter history to a specific node class |
| `last_n_runs` | `INT` | default `5`, range 1…100, step 1 | How many recent runs to include |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `node_class_filter` | `STRING` | default `""` | Node class name to filter by (e.g. 'KSampler') |
| `run_a` | `INT` | default `0` | First run number for diff mode (0 = auto-detect last two) |
| `run_b` | `INT` | default `0` | Second run number for diff mode (0 = auto-detect last two) |
| `snapshot_action` | choice: `none`, `save`, `compare`, `restore_plan` | default `"none"` | Branch snapshots. save: store the parameters of run_b (0 = the latest run) under snapshot_name; snapshots are kept when old runs are pruned. compare: differences between snapshot_name and compare_to. restore_plan: only the parameters matching restore_filter from snapshot_name (empty = latest run). Result in snapshot_json. |
| `snapshot_name` | `STRING` | default `""` | Name of the snapshot to save, compare or restore from (e.g. 'branch-A'). |
| `compare_to` | `STRING` | default `""` | compare: the second snapshot's name; empty = the latest run. |
| `restore_filter` | `STRING` | default `""` | restore_plan: comma-separated node classes, node titles, node ids, parameter names or Class.param (e.g. 'KSampler, cfg'). Only matching parameters are listed; the output is the exact patch to apply, nothing else is touched. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `history_report` | `STRING` | Human-readable parameter history report for the chosen mode. |
| 1 | `snapshot_json` | `STRING` | snapshot_action result as JSON: the saved snapshot, the differences, or the restore patch {node_id: {params}}. Empty when snapshot_action is none. |


---

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/VAE


### VAEMergeMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** VAE Merge

Merge 2 or 3 VAEs with 13 strategies (weighted_sum, sigmoid, geometric, slerp, dare_ties, …). Optional per-block weights, auto-alpha from block cosine similarity, latent-space probe to report reconstruction MSE/PSNR, dry-run, and recipe export.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/vae_merge.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vae_a` | `VAE` |  | Primary VAE (acts as base; deep-copied clone is returned). |
| `vae_b` | `VAE` |  | Secondary VAE blended into vae_a. |
| `merge_mode` | choice: `weighted_sum`, `add_difference`, `tensor_sum`, `triple_sum`, `slerp`, `sigmoid`, `geometric`, `max_abs`, … (+5) | default `"weighted_sum"` | Blend strategy. add_difference / triple_sum / smooth_add_diff need vae_c. sigmoid + geometric mimic TechnoByte/meh behaviour. distribution_xover keeps A unless B has higher detail energy. dare_ties = sparse delta + sign election. |
| `alpha` | `FLOAT` | default `0.3`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Primary blend weight. weighted_sum: 0=A, 1=B. |
| `beta` | `FLOAT` | default `0.7`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Secondary blend weight (used by add_difference / 3-VAE modes). |
| `brightness` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -1.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Post-merge brightness: an offset added to decoder.conv_out's bias (0.1 x this value in the decoder's output units). |
| `contrast` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range -1.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Post-merge contrast gain on decoder.conv_out. |
| `use_blocks` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Enable per-block sliders. When False all keys use 'alpha'. |
| `auto_alpha` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Data-driven block weights. When True, computes per-block cosine similarity between A and B; dissimilar blocks bias toward A. Overrides the manual block sliders. |
| `block_conv_in` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Weight for encoder/decoder conv_in. |
| `block_conv_out` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Weight for encoder/decoder conv_out. |
| `block_norm_out` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Weight for encoder/decoder norm_out. |
| `block_0` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | First down/up block pair. |
| `block_1` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Second down/up block pair. |
| `block_2` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Third down/up block pair. |
| `block_3` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Fourth down/up block pair (SDXL). |
| `block_mid` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | Mid block. |
| `device` | choice: `cpu`, `cuda`, `auto` | default `"cpu"` | Compute device. CPU is safe (default); CUDA is faster but uses VRAM. |
| `dry_run` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | If True, skip the merge and return only the recipe + similarity report. Use this for fast block-similarity inspection without waiting for the merge. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vae_c` | `VAE` |  | Optional third VAE for add_difference / triple_sum / smooth_add_diff. |
| `reference_image` | `IMAGE` |  | Optional reference image. If connected, encodes/decodes it through A, B, and the merged VAE; reports MSE/PSNR per VAE in probe_report. |
| `recipe_in` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | Optional recipe JSON from a previous run. When provided, overrides every widget value above so the exact merge is reproduced. |
| `block_weights_json` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | Fine-grained block weights: a JSON object {"<regex>": weight 0-1} matched against each weight's name (e.g. {"decoder\\.up\\.0\\.": 0.2}). Applied after the sliders / auto_alpha; the first matching pattern wins. Saved in the recipe. Empty = off. |
| `validation` | choice: `off`, `report`, `block_on_regression` | default `"off"` | Validation benchmark after the merge: finite / shape checks and the same five synthetic images at 64 px through A, B and the merged VAE (PSNR). report: result in validation_json and the recipe. block_on_regression: fail instead of returning a merge whose mean PSNR is more than validation_max_psnr_drop_db below the weaker input. |
| `validation_max_psnr_drop_db` | `FLOAT` | default `3.0`, range 0.0…60.0, step 0.1 | block_on_regression: allowed drop of the merged VAE's mean PSNR below min(A, B), in dB. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `vae` | `VAE` | Merged VAE (or vae_a clone when dry_run=True). |
| 1 | `info` | `STRING` | Human-readable info string summarising mode, alpha, blocks, and timing. |
| 2 | `recipe_json` | `STRING` | Reproducible recipe JSON; feed back into recipe_in to repeat the exact merge. |
| 3 | `probe_report` | `STRING` | Probe report with MSE/PSNR per VAE when reference_image is connected. |
| 4 | `weight_map` | `IMAGE` | Chart: the effective merge weight (alpha) per block group, including block_weights_json overrides, next to how similar A and B are in that block (cosine). Also on dry_run. |
| 5 | `validation_json` | `STRING` | validation on: compatibility checks and reconstruction PSNR of A, B and the merge, as JSON. Empty when off. |


---

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/VFX


### OpticalFlowMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Optical Flow Re-Vector (MEC)

Dense optical flow re-vector. RAFT primary, LK pyramid fallback. Re-vectoring is restricted to the mask region; rest is untouched.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/optical_flow.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `frame_a` | `IMAGE` |  | The frame to move (one frame or a batch). |
| `frame_b` | `IMAGE` |  | The frame to move it toward (same size and count). |
| `iters` | `INT` | default `20`, range 1…100 | RAFT refinement passes: more = finer flow, slower (the Lucas-Kanade fallback without RAFT uses its own 5). |
| `consistency_thr` | `FLOAT` | default `1.5`, range 0.0…20.0, step 0.05 | Pixels: where the forward and backward flows disagree by more than this, 'consistency' is 0. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Only re-vector inside this mask; the rest of frame_a stays. |
| `scale` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range -4.0…4.0, step 0.05 | Multiply the motion: 1 = all the way to frame_b, 0.5 = half way, -1 = the opposite way. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `re_vectored` | `IMAGE` | frame_a moved along the flow toward frame_b (only inside the mask when one is wired). |
| 1 | `flow_rgb` | `IMAGE` | The a -> b flow as a picture: red = horizontal, green = vertical (0.5 = still), blue = speed. |
| 2 | `consistency` | `MASK` | 1 where the forward and backward flows agree (reliable), 0 where they do not (occlusion, errors). |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Vault


### C2C_SecureSubgraph

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C Vault (protected subgraph)

A C2C Vault: a subgraph whose inside is encrypted. It runs with ComfyUI's own scheduler like any subgraph; opening it needs the vault password and your second factor. Create one by right-clicking a selection or a subgraph: Lock as C2C Vault / Seal as C2C Vault.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/secure_subgraph.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

### C2C_SecureSubgraphOutput

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C Vault (protected subgraph, saves)

A C2C Vault: a subgraph whose inside is encrypted. It runs with ComfyUI's own scheduler like any subgraph; opening it needs the vault password and your second factor. Create one by right-clicking a selection or a subgraph: Lock as C2C Vault / Seal as C2C Vault.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/secure_subgraph.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vault_id` | `STRING` | default `""` | Which vault this is - written when the vault is created; do not edit. |
| `vault_payload` | `STRING` | default `""` | The encrypted inside of the vault - written when it is locked or sealed; editing it breaks the vault. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `out_0` | `*` | Output 1 of the protected subgraph (the value its inside sends out here). |
| 1 | `out_1` | `*` | Output 2 of the protected subgraph (the value its inside sends out here). |

### C2C_VaultLocked

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C Vault — Locked (password to run) *(deprecated: kept so saved workflows load)*

Run a password-locked subgraph. The wiring inside travels with the workflow as AES-GCM ciphertext, so a recipient sees an opaque blob instead of your node graph.

Lock a canvas selection via right-click → C2C Vault. To run it, type the password in the field on this node and press Enter; the unlock lasts for the ComfyUI session and the password never enters the workflow JSON.

SCOPE, honestly: this stops casual inspection and copying. It cannot stop someone who can run Python in this process - the subgraph must be decrypted to execute, so the plaintext exists in memory while it runs. A lock on a door, not a safe.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/vault_node.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vault_id` | `STRING` | default `""` | Public vault handle — authenticated in the ciphertext header, never secret. Lets the UI match an unlock session to the right blob without revealing what is inside. |
| `vault_payload` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | AES-GCM ciphertext of the internal graph. This is what travels in the .json — opaque to anyone without the password. Do not hand-edit. |
| `vault_interface` | `STRING` | default `"{}"`, multiline | Clear-text manifest: socket names, types, node count, and any promoted parameters. This is the public API of the vault and it travels in the .json, so it carries a promoted parameter's LABEL and value only - which internal widget it drives is inside the ciphertext, because naming it here would describe the contents. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `input_0` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 0 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[0] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_1` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 1 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[1] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_2` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 2 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[2] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_3` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 3 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[3] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_4` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 4 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[4] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_5` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 5 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[5] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_6` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 6 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[6] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_7` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 7 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[7] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_8` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 8 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[8] inside the ciphertext. |
| `input_9` | `*` |  | Positional boundary input 9 — the label you see is applied by the vault interface manifest, not by this socket name. Index must match boundary_in[9] inside the ciphertext. |
| `param_0` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 0 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_1` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 1 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_2` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 2 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_3` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 3 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_4` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 4 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_5` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 5 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_6` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 6 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_7` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 7 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |

| # | Name | Type | What it is |

### C2C_VaultSealed

**Shown in the menu as:** C2C Vault — Sealed (runs without password) *(deprecated: kept so saved workflows load)*

Sealed subgraph - RUNS WITHOUT A PASSWORD, so you can hand a workflow to someone and they just use it. The password is only needed to OPEN the vault for editing.

Sealed for installs (the default): it runs without a password only on this install and on the installs whose public keys you pasted when you sealed it; each install keeps its own private key in the OS credential store. Portable seal: runs on any install with this pack, so it is obfuscation only. A legacy (v1) seal still runs and is marked; open it and lock it again to upgrade.

HONEST SCOPE: a recipient install can always decrypt what it runs, so sealing stops reading and copying your graph out of the .json, not a determined extractor on a recipient machine. Use C2C_VaultLocked when the recipient should need your password to run it at all.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/vault_node.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

| `vault_interface` | `STRING` | default `"{}"`, multiline | Clear-text manifest: socket names, types, node count, and any promoted parameters. This is the public API of the vault and it travels in the .json, so it carries a promoted parameter's LABEL and value only - which internal widget it drives is inside the ciphertext, because naming it here would describe the contents. |

| `param_5` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 5 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_6` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 6 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |
| `param_7` | `*` |  | Promoted parameter 7 — connect this only when a promoted parameter has been converted to an input. The vault decides which internal widget it drives; that mapping lives inside the ciphertext and is never in the workflow JSON. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `output_0` | `*` | — |
| 1 | `output_1` | `*` | — |
| 2 | `output_2` | `*` | — |
| 3 | `output_3` | `*` | — |
| 4 | `output_4` | `*` | — |
| 5 | `output_5` | `*` | — |
| 6 | `output_6` | `*` | — |
| 7 | `output_7` | `*` | — |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Video


### AVHandlesMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** AV Handles — Add / Trim

Add repeated frames at the head (or tail) to stabilise a video model, then trim exactly those back off afterwards — without retyping the number. In trim mode it uses the count the add recorded, so the two ends cannot drift apart. Audio is moved by the matching duration.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/av_handles.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | choice: `add`, `trim`, `fit` | default `"fit"` | fit — say how many frames you want OUT. The node works out what the model will accept, pads up to it, and remembers the target so the trim lands exactly there. This is the one to use. add — put a specific number of frames on, and remember how many. trim — take them back off, using the remembered target or count. |
| `handle_frames` | `INT` | default `8`, range 0…400, step 1 | ADD: how many frames to put on. With a grid selected, 0 means 'just enough to land on the grid'. TRIM: ignored unless trim_amount is 'manual'. |
| `trim_amount` | choice: `auto`, `manual` | default `"auto"` | TRIM mode only. auto — use the count the add side recorded (or the one wired into `handles`). This is the point of the node: the two ends cannot drift apart. manual — use handle_frames instead. Only for undoing handles this node did not add. It is a separate control because 'left at the default' and 'deliberately typed 8' cannot be told apart in one number, and guessing wrong silently trims the wrong amount. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `images` | `IMAGE` |  | The clip to pad or trim (fit mode needs it). Wire images, audio or both. |
| `audio` | `AUDIO` |  | Audio moved by the same duration as the frames, so it stays in sync. |
| `target_frames` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…100000, step 1 | FIT mode: how many frames you want OUT. The model's own grid is an implementation detail — ask for 64 and you get 64, whatever the model needs to render to make them. 0 in fit mode means 'use the clip's current length as the target', which pads it to something the model accepts and trims straight back. |
| `model` | choice: `none (any frame count)`, `MiniMax H3 (17n+5)`, `Wan 2.x (4n+1)`, `LTX / LTX-2 (8n+1)`, `Hunyuan Video (4n+1)`, `CogVideoX (8n+1)`, `Mochi (6n+1)`, `custom (step / plus)` | default `"MiniMax H3 (17n+5)"` | FIT, and ADD with padding_mode 'model preset': which model's frame grid to land on. Every video model wants step*n + plus frames and they disagree only on the numbers: H3 is 17n+5, Wan is 4n+1, LTX is 8n+1. Hand a model a count it does not accept and it does not complain — it reinterprets the clip, and the result comes out at the wrong speed. |
| `custom_step` | `INT` | default `4`, range 1…1000 | Only used when model is 'custom'. |
| `custom_plus` | `INT` | default `1`, range 0…1000 | Only used when model is 'custom'. |
| `side` | choice: `head`, `tail`, `both` | default `"head"` | Which end. Handles go on the head because that is the run a video model uses to settle. both: handle_frames on the head AND the tail (fit splits its padding across the two ends). TRIM takes a wired or remembered count off exactly the ends it went on; this setting only matters for a manual count. |
| `padding_mode` | choice: `disabled`, `model preset`, `WAN (4n+1)`, `LTX2 (8n+1)`, `H3 (17n+5)` | default `"model preset"` | ADD: round the total onto a model's frame grid. A model that wants 4n+1 and is handed 4n does not complain — it reinterprets the clip, and the result comes out at the wrong speed. model preset: the grid of the `model` dropdown (or the custom step / plus). disabled: exactly handle_frames, no rounding. The fixed WAN / LTX2 / H3 entries are kept for workflows saved with them. |
| `manual_fps` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…240.0, step 0.001 | 0 measures it from the clip's own audio. Set it when there is no audio to measure against — a guessed 30 on 24fps material puts the silence 25% out. |
| `handles` | `HANDLES` |  | Wire the add node's `handles` output here for an exact, explicit count. Takes priority over the remembered one, and works across separate workflows where the memory cannot. |
| `memo_key` | `STRING` | default `""` | Only needed when two independent clips are being handled in one workflow — give each its own name so their counts do not overwrite each other. Empty is right for one clip. |
| `handle_align` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | ADD only. Round each end's handle count UP to a whole multiple of the `model` grid step (Wan 4, LTX 8, H3 17, or custom_step), so the handles fill whole latent time blocks and a clip that was on the grid is on it again after the trim. If a padding grid is also on, its rounding frames go on the tail. Off: handle_frames exactly, as before. |
| `audio_sync` | choice: `frame duration`, `exact samples` | default `"frame duration"` | frame duration: audio moves by frames / fps on each end (as before). exact samples: the add records the exact samples it inserted (in `handles` and the memory), and the trim removes exactly those when it undoes the same ends - the audio comes back sample for sample even when the trim side measures a slightly different fps. Set it on the add AND the trim. |
| `audio_fade_ms` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1000.0, step 1.0 | 0 = off. Linear fades of this length at each audio edge the node makes: add / fit fade the original audio in after the head silence and out before the tail silence; trim fades in at the new start and out at the new end (only on ends that were changed). Removes clicks. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `images` | `IMAGE` | Frames, with handles added or removed. |
| 1 | `audio` | `AUDIO` | Audio moved by the same duration, so it stays in step. |
| 2 | `total_frames` | `INT` | How many frames came out. |
| 3 | `handles` | `HANDLES` | The handle count, to wire into the trim node. Exact, and it does not depend on the memory — use it when the add and the trim are in different workflows. |
| 4 | `info` | `STRING` | What happened, including WHERE the trim count came from. |

### LoadImagesPathC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Load Images Path (C2C)

Load a folder of numbered images as frames, in name order, with a cap, a skip and every-Nth selection; pick an EXR layer by name, and read what the folder holds (patterns, frame ranges, missing frames) in sequence_report. The folder must be inside ComfyUI's input / output / media roots.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_video/nodes_load.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load — HallettVisual (Matthew Hallett) (https://github.com/HallettVisual/ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load) - per CREDITS.md

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `directory` | `STRING` |  | Directory of sequential images. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image_load_cap` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Max images; 0 = no cap. |
| `skip_first_images` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Skip this many images first. |
| `select_every_nth` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…100000 | Keep every Nth image. |
| `exr_layer` | `STRING` | default `""` | EXR only. Empty: the first channels of the first part (what this node always loaded). auto: the main picture (rgba). Or a layer name - diffuse, depth, N, crypto00 ... (sequence_report lists them): that layer goes to IMAGE, data layers (depth, normals, position, motion) as raw values. exr_passes carries every layer. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `IMAGE` | `IMAGE` | The frames. |
| 1 | `MASK` | `MASK` | 1 - alpha of each frame when the images have alpha. |
| 2 | `frame_count` | `INT` | Frames loaded. |
| 3 | `video` | `C2C_VIDEO` | The lazy media handle for the C2C video nodes. |
| 4 | `exr_passes` | `C2C_EXR_PASSES` | Every layer of an EXR source, lazily, for the same frames (empty for other images). |
| 5 | `sequence_report` | `STRING` | JSON: the sequences found in the folder (pattern, frame range, step, zero padding, missing frames), files ignored, which files load, and for an EXR its layers and what the IMAGE's values mean. Linked alone it reads no pixels. |


---

### LoadVideoC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Load Video (C2C)

Load a video, an image sequence or an EXR sequence as frames - streamed, not decoded all at once - with VHS-style frame rate, size, cap, skip and every-Nth controls, audio, alpha as a mask, and a frame map back to the source frame numbers. Connect a VAE to also get the frames encoded on the latent output; IMAGE stays the frames.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_video/nodes_load.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load — HallettVisual (Matthew Hallett) (https://github.com/HallettVisual/ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load) - per CREDITS.md

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `video` | choice: `example.png` |  | A video, an image, an EXR or an image sequence (shot.####.exr) from the ComfyUI input folder - upload with the node's bar or drop files on it. Server path mode (location) reads any folder instead. |
| `force_rate` | `FLOAT` | default `0`, range 0…240, step 1 | Output FPS; 0 keeps source. |
| `custom_width` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…16384 | Output width; 0 keeps source or derives from height. |
| `custom_height` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…16384 | Output height; 0 keeps source or derives from width. |
| `frame_load_cap` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Max frames; 0 = no cap. |
| `skip_first_frames` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Skip this many frames first. |
| `select_every_nth` | `INT` | default `1`, range 1…100000 | Keep every Nth frame. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vae` | `VAE` |  | Connect to fill the latent output. |
| `format` | choice: `None`, `AnimateDiff`, `Mochi`, `LTXV`, `Hunyuan`, `Cosmos`, `Wan`, `MiniMax H3` | default `"None"` | Model preset (frame grid / dim multiple). |
| `crop_x` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.001 | Crop, as a fraction of the frame (x, y, width, height; 0, 0, 1, 1 = no crop). Set it with the Crop tool in the player; custom_width / custom_height then resize the cropped frame. |
| `crop_y` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.001 | Crop, as a fraction of the frame (x, y, width, height; 0, 0, 1, 1 = no crop). Set it with the Crop tool in the player; custom_width / custom_height then resize the cropped frame. |
| `crop_w` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.001 | Crop, as a fraction of the frame (x, y, width, height; 0, 0, 1, 1 = no crop). Set it with the Crop tool in the player; custom_width / custom_height then resize the cropped frame. |
| `crop_h` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.001 | Crop, as a fraction of the frame (x, y, width, height; 0, 0, 1, 1 = no crop). Set it with the Crop tool in the player; custom_width / custom_height then resize the cropped frame. |
| `decoder` | choice: `auto`, `fast`, `exact` | default `"auto"` | auto: the fast path only where it is faster with the same colour - measured, that is the exact reader on the tested machine. fast: ffmpeg decodes 8-bit 4:2:0 video (the same frames; on an NVIDIA GPU the colour conversion runs there, mean 0.5/255 from the exact reader). exact: the frame-accurate PyAV reader (10-bit, HDR, alpha, ProRes and image sequences always use it). |
| `seconds_cap` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…86400.0, step 0.1 | Load at most this many seconds after skip_first_frames (0 = no limit), at the force_rate rate when set. Works with frame_load_cap: the smaller one wins, and a format preset then snaps the count. |
| `resolution` | choice: `source`, `2160p (4K)`, `1440p`, `1080p`, `720p`, `480p`, `2048 wide`, `1920 wide`, … (+5) | default `"custom"` | Output size by its LONGEST side, keeping the aspect: 2160p is 3840, 1080p is 1920. custom uses custom_width / custom_height (0 = keep the source size or follow the aspect). source ignores them. proxy 1/2, 1/4, 1/8: the source (after the crop) at that fraction of its size, read straight at that size for a fast, light look-dev pass; video_info and info record the full size. |
| `divisible_by` | choice: `1`, `2`, `8`, `16`, `32`, `64` | default `1` | Round both output dimensions to a multiple of this. A format preset or a VAE may raise it. |
| `audio_when_missing` | choice: `silence`, `none` | default `"silence"` | When the file has no audio: silence outputs a silent track as long as the loaded frames (what the loader always did, so downstream nodes keep working); none outputs nothing. |
| `handles` | choice: `off`, `fit`, `add` | default `"off"` | Handles = extra frames for a video model to settle on, added here and trimmed off again by Save Video (C2C) (it reads them from video_info). fit: pad the clip UP to the model's frame grid (handle_target = frames you want out, 0 = this clip's length). add: put handle_frames on, rounded onto the grid. Repeated edge frames, never black; the audio moves with them. |
| `handle_frames` | `INT` | default `8`, range 0…400 | add: frames to put on (side both: on EACH end). With a grid, 0 = just enough to land on it. |
| `handle_target` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | fit: how many frames you want out after the trim. 0 = this clip's own length. |
| `handle_side` | choice: `head`, `tail`, `both` | default `"head"` | Which end gets the handles. head is where a video model settles; both splits them. |
| `handle_grid` | choice: `from format`, `none (any frame count)`, `MiniMax H3 (17n+5)`, `Wan 2.x (4n+1)`, `LTX / LTX-2 (8n+1)`, `Hunyuan Video (4n+1)`, `CogVideoX (8n+1)`, `Mochi (6n+1)`, … (+1) | default `"from format"` | The model's frame grid: 'from format' uses the format preset above (MiniMax H3 17n+5, Wan 4n+1, LTXV 8n+1 ...), so there is one grid, not two; or pick one here. |
| `handle_custom_step` | `INT` | default `4`, range 1…1000 | custom grid: step (frames = step * n + plus). |
| `handle_custom_plus` | `INT` | default `1`, range 0…1000 | custom grid: plus. |
| `colorspace_in` | choice: `off (as before)`, `auto (detect)`, `ACES2065-1`, `ACEScc`, `ACEScct`, `ACEScg`, `ADX10`, `ADX16`, … (+49) | default `"off (as before)"` | The colour space this file was rendered in. auto: read it from the file (a .colorspace / .c2c.json sidecar, the EXR colorInteropID or chromaticities, the DPX or movie colour tags). An EXR with none is the OCIO config's scene_linear role (Nuke's working space); an untagged movie or image is display. Pick a space to override. off: no colour management (what the loader always did). |
| `working_space` | choice: `sRGB display (keep Rec.709 / sRGB)`, `preserve source`, `ACES2065-1`, `ACEScc`, `ACEScct`, `ACEScg`, `ADX10`, `ADX16`, … (+49) | default `"sRGB display (keep Rec.709 / sRGB)"` | What the frames are converted to for the models. sRGB display: a Rec.709 / sRGB source stays as it is; anything else goes through the config's view (tone-mapped: ACES 2.0 SDR in the built-in config, what Nuke's viewer shows). Save Video (C2C) reverses exactly this. preserve source: no conversion. |
| `name_prefix` | `STRING` | default `""` | Save Video (C2C) on 'auto (from Load)' names its file name_prefix + this clip's name + name_suffix, e.g. proj_ + shot010 + _comp; with the version folder the name carries the folder's version. Empty = nothing added. Typing a name on Save wins. |
| `name_suffix` | `STRING` | default `""` | Save Video (C2C) on 'auto (from Load)' names its file name_prefix + this clip's name + name_suffix, e.g. proj_ + shot010 + _comp; with the version folder the name carries the folder's version. Empty = nothing added. Typing a name on Save wins. |
| `extra_handles` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…100 | More handles on top of fit / add, in whole steps of the model's frame grid (MiniMax H3 17, Wan 4, LTX 8 ...), so the clip stays on the grid; on handle_side (both splits them). The details line shows k x step = frames and the total. Save Video (C2C) trims them with the rest. |
| `location` | choice: `input folder`, `server path` | default `"input folder"` | Where the source is. input folder: pick or upload it in 'video'. server path: type any path in 'path' (a clip, an image, a folder or a pattern like shot.####.exr) - what Load Video Path (C2C) did. |
| `path` | `STRING` | default `""` | server path mode: a clip, an image, an EXR, a folder of images, or a sequence pattern (shot.####.exr, shot.%04d.exr, shot.$F4.exr, shot.[1001-1100].exr). One frame of a sequence finds the whole sequence. |
| `source_mode` | choice: `auto`, `video file`, `image sequence`, `EXR sequence`, `single image`, `single EXR` | default `"auto"` | auto: a movie is a movie; a numbered image finds its sequence; a lone image is one frame. Force it when that guess is wrong: single image / single EXR loads exactly one file even when it has siblings (frame_range picks which frame of a sequence). |
| `sequence_fps` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…240.0, step 0.001 | Rate of an image sequence or still. 0 = the file's own rate (EXR FramesPerSecond, DPX frame rate), else 24. A still is one frame at this rate - never a longer clip. |
| `frame_range` | `STRING` | default `""` | Source frame NUMBERS (Nuke style): 1001-1100, 1001-1100x2 (every 2nd), or one frame like 1050. Empty = all. For a movie, frame numbers count from 0. |
| `reverse` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Play the selected frames last to first (the audio too). |
| `frame_offset` | `INT` | default `0`, range -10000000…10000000 | Shifts the frame numbers frame_map reports for the output (where frame 0 lands on your timeline). The pixels do not move. |
| `missing_frames` | choice: `error`, `black`, `hold previous`, `nearest`, `skip (as before)` | default `"error"` | A frame inside the sequence's range with no file. error (default) stops and names them; black, hold previous and nearest fill the gap and say so in info; skip (as before) drops it - every later frame then plays early. |
| `edge_policy` | choice: `hold`, `black`, `loop`, `bounce`, `error` | default `"hold"` | frame_range beyond the first / last frame: hold repeats the edge frame, black adds black frames, loop wraps, bounce plays back and forth, error stops. |
| `exr_layer` | `STRING` | default `"auto"` | The EXR layer the IMAGE shows: auto (the beauty / rgba), or a layer name from info - diffuse, depth, N, P, crypto00 ... Data layers (depth, normals, position, motion) load as raw values, never colour-converted. exr_passes carries every layer. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `IMAGE` | `IMAGE` | The frames (always IMAGE, with or without a VAE). |
| 1 | `frame_count` | `INT` | Frames loaded. |
| 2 | `audio` | `AUDIO` | The audio of the loaded frames. |
| 3 | `video_info` | `VHS_VIDEOINFO` | Source and loaded numbers (VHS-compatible). |
| 4 | `video` | `C2C_VIDEO` | The lazy media handle: nothing decoded until a node reads a window. |
| 5 | `mask` | `MASK` | 1 - alpha, when the source has alpha. |
| 6 | `info` | `STRING` | MEDIA_INFO: what the source is and exactly what loaded (JSON). |
| 7 | `frame_map` | `STRING` | Output frame -> source frame and its frame NUMBER (h3.frame_ledger v1.1 JSON; -1 = a black frame). |
| 8 | `exr_passes` | `C2C_EXR_PASSES` | Every EXR layer, lazily (empty for other sources). |
| 9 | `metadata` | `STRING` | The source's header / container metadata (JSON). |
| 10 | `latent` | `LATENT` | LATENT: the frames encoded with the connected VAE (empty without a VAE; connecting this without a VAE is an error). |

### MaskTrackerMEC

**Shown in the menu as:** Mask Track — Motion/Propagate/Anchor/Consistency/Stabilize

Unified video-mask tracker. Pick a mode and the corresponding engine runs. All modes share the (mask, video) input pair. Heavy work is chunked/vectorized; CPU fallback always available.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/mask_tracker_mec.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | choice: `motion`, `propagate`, `anchor`, `consistency_check`, `stabilize`, `spline_track`, `bbox_smooth` | default `"motion"` | motion: per-frame motion mask (pixel/flow/bg/hist). propagate: seed mask on one frame, push to all frames. anchor: SDF interpolation between anchor masks. consistency_check: score flicker between consecutive frames. stabilize: smooth a mask batch over time and flag frames that drop or jump. spline_track: follow spline keyframes through the video (Lucas-Kanade). bbox_smooth: smooth a list of boxes over time. |
| `camera_compensation` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [motion] subtract global camera motion |
| `stabilization_method` | choice: `homography`, `affine`, `translation` | default `"homography"` | [motion] camera-motion model |
| `detection_mode` | choice: `combined`, `pixel_diff`, `optical_flow`, `background_sub`, `histogram_diff` | default `"combined"` | [motion] active method(s) |
| `pixel_diff_enabled` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [motion] enable pixel-diff method |
| `pixel_diff_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.05`, range 0.001…1.0, step 0.001 | [motion] pixel-diff threshold |
| `flow_enabled` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [motion] enable optical flow |
| `flow_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.1…50.0, step 0.1 | [motion] flow magnitude threshold |
| `flow_algorithm` | choice: `farneback`, `phase_correlation` | default `"farneback"` | [motion] flow algorithm |
| `bg_sub_enabled` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | [motion] background subtraction |
| `bg_model_frames` | `INT` | default `5`, range 1…30, step 1 | [motion] frames for bg model |
| `bg_sub_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.1`, range 0.001…1.0, step 0.001 | [motion] bg-diff threshold |
| `hist_enabled` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | [motion] histogram diff |
| `hist_grid_size` | `INT` | default `16`, range 4…64, step 4 | [motion] histogram grid NxN |
| `hist_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.15`, range 0.01…1.0, step 0.01 | [motion] histogram L2 threshold |
| `combine_method` | choice: `union`, `intersection` | default `"union"` | [motion] method combination |
| `grow_pixels` | `FLOAT` | default `4.0`, range 0.0…64.0, step 1.0 | [motion] dilate result |
| `min_region_size` | `INT` | default `100`, range 0…10000, step 10 | [motion] noise filter |
| `temporal_smooth` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [motion] gaussian time smoothing |
| `source_frame` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…99999 | [propagate] frame where mask is drawn |
| `propagate_mode` | choice: `static`, `optical_flow`, `sam2_video`, `fade`, `scale_linear` | default `"static"` | [propagate] propagation method |
| `prop_flow_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `2.0`, range 0.0…50.0, step 0.5 | [propagate] optical-flow threshold |
| `fade_start` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | [propagate] opacity at source frame |
| `fade_end` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | [propagate] opacity at last frame |
| `bidirectional` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [propagate] forward+backward from source |
| `anchor_frames` | `STRING` | default `"0"` | [anchor] CSV frame indices for each anchor mask |
| `total_frames` | `INT` | default `30`, range 1…99999 | [anchor] total output frames |
| `easing` | choice: `linear`, `ease_in`, `ease_out`, `smooth_step` | default `"smooth_step"` | [anchor] easing curve |
| `sdf_iterations` | `INT` | default `64`, range 4…512, step 4 | [anchor] SDF diffusion iterations |
| `flow_refinement` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | [anchor] optical-flow refine (needs video) |
| `metric` | choice: `mask_iou`, `pixel_diff`, `flow_warp`, `edge_stability` | default `"pixel_diff"` | [consistency_check] metric |
| `binarize_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.01…0.99, step 0.01 | [consistency_check] mask binarize threshold |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mask` | `MASK` |  | Required by propagate (seed), anchor (anchor stack), consistency_check (mask_iou). Optional for motion. |
| `video` | `IMAGE` |  | Video frame batch (B,H,W,C). Required by motion, propagate, anchor flow_refinement, and pixel/flow consistency. |
| `sam_model` | `SAM_MODEL` |  | [propagate sam2_video mode] SAM2 model |
| `points_json` | `STRING` | default `""`, multiline | [propagate sam2_video mode] point prompts |
| `stabilize_temporal_mode` | choice: `none`, `gaussian`, `raft_flow` | default `"none"` | [stabilize] none = integrity report only; gaussian = smooth across frames; raft_flow = blend each frame with the flow-warped previous one. |
| `stabilize_blend` | `FLOAT` | default `0.5`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | [stabilize] mix factor: 1.0 = pure warped-previous, 0.0 = current only. |
| `stabilize_sigma` | `FLOAT` | default `1.0`, range 0.0…8.0, step 0.1 | [stabilize] gaussian sigma (gaussian only). |
| `stabilize_device` | choice: `cuda`, `cpu` | default `"cuda"` | [stabilize] where to run. |
| `stabilize_drop_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.4`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | [stabilize] area ratio / IoU below this flags the frame. |
| `stabilize_jump_threshold` | `FLOAT` | default `0.15`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.01 | [stabilize] centroid jump (normalized) above this flags the frame. |
| `spline_data` | `STRING` | default `"[]"` | [spline_track] keyframes from the spline tracker canvas: [{frame:int, points:[[x,y],…]}, …]. |
| `spline_closed` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | [spline_track] closed loop (a filled shape) vs open path (a stroke). |
| `spline_samples_per_segment` | `INT` | default `20`, range 2…128, step 1 | [spline_track] curve resolution per segment. |
| `spline_tracking_weight` | `FLOAT` | default `0.7`, range 0.0…1.0, step 0.05 | [spline_track] blend between the tracker and the keyframe interpolation (1 = pure Lucas-Kanade, 0 = pure interpolation). |
| `spline_klt_window` | `INT` | default `21`, range 5…51, step 2 | [spline_track] Lucas-Kanade window size. |
| `spline_feather_radius` | `FLOAT` | default `0.0`, range 0.0…64.0, step 0.5 | [spline_track] gaussian edge feather. |
| `spline_stroke_width` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…64, step 1 | [spline_track] stroke width of an open spline. |
| `bbox_json` | `STRING` | default `"[]"`, multiline | [bbox_smooth] JSON array of [x,y,w,h], one per frame. |
| `bbox_radius` | `INT` | default `3`, range 1…30, step 1 | [bbox_smooth] smoothing window radius in frames. |
| `bbox_method` | choice: `median_then_exponential`, `moving_average`, `exponential`, `median` | default `"median_then_exponential"` | [bbox_smooth] smoothing strategy. |
| `bbox_alpha` | `FLOAT` | default `0.3`, range 0.05…1.0, step 0.05 | [bbox_smooth] exponential factor. |
| `allow_download` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | [stabilize, raft_flow] let the node download torchvision's RAFT-small weights (about 4 MB) if they are not on this machine yet. Off: an existing copy is used; none = a plain message. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `masks` | `MASK` | Per-frame mask batch (B,H,W). |
| 1 | `preview` | `IMAGE` | Preview overlay (propagate) or video passthrough. |
| 2 | `score` | `FLOAT` | Mode-specific scalar: motion intensity / mean confidence / flicker score. |
| 3 | `info_json` | `STRING` | Mode-specific JSON diagnostic payload. |
| 4 | `metric` | `STRING` | Mode/metric label string. |
| 5 | `warning` | `STRING` | [stabilize] frames whose mask dropped or jumped, in words; empty when none (and in the other modes). |
| 6 | `bboxes` | `BBOX` | [bbox_smooth] the smoothed boxes, one [x,y,w,h] per frame; empty in the other modes. |
| 7 | `primary_bbox` | `BBOX` | [bbox_smooth] the first smoothed box; [0,0,0,0] in the other modes. |
| 8 | `spline_data_out` | `SPLINE_DATA` | The spline keyframes (spline_data), passed through. |

### SaveVideoC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Save Video (C2C)

Save an IMAGE batch as H.264/H.265/ProRes/DNxHR/FFV1/WebM/GIF/WebP or 16-bit PNG / EXR sequences. Streams chunk-by-chunk within the RAM budget, muxes audio, applies OCIO colour transforms, and shows a browser preview with timeline scrub after each run.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/save_video.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `format` | choice: `custom (container / codec)`, `MP4 H.264`, `MP4 H.265 10-bit`, `MOV ProRes 422`, `MOV ProRes 422 HQ`, `MOV ProRes 4444`, `MOV ProRes 4444 XQ`, `MOV DNxHR HQ`, … (+14) | default `"custom (container / codec)"` | custom: choose container, codec, profile, bit depth and chroma below. The other entries are the old single list, kept so saved workflows encode as before. |
| `fps` | `FLOAT` | default `24.0`, range 0.001…240.0, step 0.001 | Frame rate for the written file. When video_info is wired and fps is left at 24, loaded_fps from the loader is used. |
| `filename_prefix` | `STRING` | default `"C2C/video"` | File name start; may include folders. Folder Version Incrementer naming uses the version name instead. 'auto (from Load)' = the followed Load Video's name. |
| `quality` | `INT` | default `80`, range 0…100, step 1 | Encoder quality where the format supports it (CRF / qscale / ProRes profile). Hidden for lossless formats. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `images` | `IMAGE` |  | The frames to save. Or wire latent + vae instead to decode and save as one stream. |
| `audio` | `AUDIO` |  | Muxed into the file when the container takes audio. |
| `video_info` | `VHS_VIDEOINFO` |  | From Load Video (C2C): frame rate, handles and colour of the source. Without it the node follows the images back to their Load Video. |
| `colorspace_in` | choice: `auto (from Load)`, `ACES2065-1`, `ACEScc`, `ACEScct`, `ACEScg`, `ADX10`, `ADX16`, `ARRI LogC3 (EI800)`, … (+48) | default `"sRGB - Display"` | OCIO colour space the frames are in. auto (from Load) = the space Load Video (C2C) gave them, so Save reverses exactly what Load did. |
| `colorspace_out` | choice: `same as input`, `auto (from Load)`, `ACES2065-1`, `ACEScc`, `ACEScct`, `ACEScg`, `ADX10`, `ADX16`, … (+49) | default `"same as input"` | OCIO colour space written to the file. same as input = no conversion; auto (from Load) = the source file's own space. |
| `naming` | choice: `default`, `ComfyUI counter`, `Folder Version Incrementer`, `Folder Version` | default `"default"` | Folder Version Incrementer: the version folder and name come from the Folder Version Incrementer node in this graph (no wire needed), or from the incrementer's defaults when there is none; a wired subfolder still wins. ComfyUI counter: prefix_00001 like Save Image. default: the C2C setting (Folder Version Incrementer unless you changed it). |
| `naming_resolved` | `STRING` | default `""` | Set by the UI from c2c.saveVideo.naming. |
| `subfolder` | `STRING` | default `""` | Folder inside the output folder. When set it wins over the Folder Version Incrementer folder. |
| `save_output` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | On: write to the output folder. Off: write a temp file for the preview only (no version folder). |
| `latent` | `LATENT` |  | Stream save: a video latent decoded straight into the file, in order, keeping the VAE's causal state - the full frame batch is never held in RAM. Wire this and vae instead of images. |
| `vae` | `VAE` |  | The VAE for latent (stream save). |
| `tile_mode` | choice: `auto`, `off`, `manual` | default `"auto"` | Stream save, Wan VAEs: auto tiles only when the frame would not fit the GPU (2K on 8 GB -> 512 px). Each tile is decoded over the whole clip in one causal pass and blended on disk, so the clip never sits in RAM. off: no tiles. manual: tile_size. |
| `tile_size` | `INT` | default `512`, range 64…2048, step 64 | Tile size in pixels when tile_mode is manual. |
| `save_metadata` | `BOOLEAN` | default `True` | Write '<name>.png' next to the saved file: its first frame with the workflow and prompt inside - drag it into ComfyUI to rebuild the graph. The video itself never carries the workflow. |
| `container` | choice: `MP4`, `MOV`, `MKV`, `WebM`, `GIF`, `WebP`, `Image sequence` | default `"MP4"` | The file type. Used when format is custom. |
| `codec` | choice: `H.264`, `H.265`, `AV1 (SVT)`, `H.264 (NVENC)`, `H.265 (NVENC)`, `AV1 (NVENC)`, `ProRes`, `DNxHR`, … (+14) | default `"H.264"` | The video codec; the list follows the container. |
| `profile` | choice: `High`, `Main`, `Baseline`, `High 10`, `High 4:2:2`, `High 4:4:4`, `Main 10`, `Main 12`, … (+36) | default `"High"` | The codec's profile (ProRes 422 HQ, DNxHR HQX, H.265 Main 10, EXR ZIP ...). |
| `bit_depth` | choice: `8`, `10`, `12`, `16`, `32 (float)` | default `"8"` | Bits per channel the profile allows. EXR: 16 = half float, 32 = float. |
| `chroma` | choice: `4:2:0`, `4:2:2`, `4:4:4`, `RGB` | default `"4:2:0"` | Chroma subsampling: 4:2:0 (delivery), 4:2:2 (edit), 4:4:4 / RGB (full). |
| `color_primaries` | choice: `auto`, `BT.709`, `BT.2020`, `Display P3`, `BT.601` | default `"auto"` | Colour primaries written into the file. auto: from colorspace_out (sRGB / Rec.709 named, others untagged). For YUV codecs the conversion matrix follows (BT.2020 -> BT.2020 NCL). |
| `color_transfer` | choice: `auto`, `BT.709`, `sRGB`, `linear`, `PQ (ST 2084)`, `HLG`, `gamma 2.2` | default `"auto"` | Transfer function written into the file (it labels; the pixels come from colorspace_out). |
| `color_range` | choice: `auto`, `limited`, `full` | default `"auto"` | YUV codecs: limited (16-235, broadcast, the default) or full (0-255). Changes the conversion and the tag together. RGB codecs are always full range. |
| `handles_trim` | choice: `auto`, `manual`, `off` | default `"auto"` | auto: trim exactly the handles Load Video (C2C) added (read from video_info; fit's target length wins). manual: trim_frames on trim_side. off: write every frame that arrives. |
| `trim_frames` | `INT` | default `8`, range 0…100000 | manual trim: frames to cut (side both: on each end). |
| `trim_side` | choice: `head`, `tail`, `both` | default `"head"` | manual trim: which end. |
| `range_in` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…10000000 | Export range, first frame (of the trimmed clip). Set it with the player's In handle. 0 / 0 = the whole clip. |
| `range_out` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…10000000 | Export range, last frame (inclusive). 0 = to the end. |
| `write_mode` | choice: `write`, `preview first` | default `"write"` | preview first: a run shows a quick preview of exactly what would be written (handles trimmed, range applied) and writes nothing; the player's Write file button then writes it - only the encode runs again, the graph above is cached. |
| `write_token` | `STRING` | default `""` | Set by the player's Write file button. |
| `format_family` | choice: `Video`, `Image Sequence`, `Custom` | default `"Video"` | Video, Image Sequence, or Custom (container -> codec -> profile -> bit depth -> chroma). |
| `subformat` | choice: `from the controls`, `H.264 / MP4`, `H.265 / HEVC / MP4`, `ProRes 422 Proxy`, `ProRes 422 LT`, `ProRes 422`, `ProRes 422 HQ`, `ProRes 4444`, … (+8) | default `"from the controls"` | The output format. It fixes the container and codec; only its own options show. 'from the controls' = the five controls exactly (saved workflows). |
| `encoder_preset` | choice: `default`, `ultrafast`, `superfast`, `veryfast`, `faster`, `fast`, `medium`, `slow`, … (+2) | default `"default"` | H.264 / H.265 speed vs size (NVENC and AV1 get the matching step). default = the encoder's own. |
| `gop` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…10000 | Keyframe interval in frames (0 = the encoder's own). 1 = every frame a keyframe (edit-friendly). |
| `bitrate_kbps` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…2000000 | Average bitrate in kbit/s instead of constant quality (0 = constant quality from quality). |
| `channels` | choice: `auto`, `RGB`, `RGBA` | default `"auto"` | auto: alpha when the frames have it. RGB: never alpha. RGBA: alpha always (opaque when the frames have none) - where the format can store it. |
| `png_compression` | `INT` | default `-1`, range -1…9 | PNG zlib level 0 (fast, big) - 9 (slow, small); -1 = the writer's default. Lossless either way. |
| `follow_load` | `STRING` | default `"auto"` | Which Load Video this Save takes handles, frame rate, colour and name from. auto: the one reached through each node's first image input; a node id (the picker on the node) pins that one. A wired video_info always wins. |
| `review_copy` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Also write '<name>_review.mp4' next to the file: H.264, half size, in the OCIO display (sRGB - Display), with the burn-in below. For review only - the master gets no burn-in. |
| `burn_in` | choice: `standard`, `custom`, `off` | default `"standard"` | standard: name + version (top left), frame + timecode (top right), colour spaces (bottom left), date (bottom right). custom: burn_in_template. off: none. |
| `burn_in_template` | `STRING` | default `"{shot} {version}|{frame}  {tc}|{colour}|{date}"` | Four corners, top-left \| top-right \| bottom-left \| bottom-right, with {shot} {version} {frame} {tc} {date} {artist} {colour}. The frame and timecode are the source's when Save carried them. |
| `burn_in_artist` | `STRING` | default `""` | What {artist} writes in the custom template. |
| `pingpong` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Bounce the clip for a seamless loop: forward, then backward without repeating the two turnaround frames (n frames -> 2n-2). Audio is bounced with the picture. Applied after the handles trim and the export range (the 'preview first' preview shows the clip unbounced). Needs an IMAGE batch, not a streamed latent save; source frame numbers and timecode are not carried into the file when on. |
| `write_manifest` | `BOOLEAN` | default `False` | Also write '<name>.manifest.json' next to the file ('<folder>.manifest.json' for an image sequence): which source frames were written (after handles, range, pingpong), the audio, the codec / profile / encoder settings, the OCIO colour transform and colour tags, and an output check that reads the written file back (frame count, size, frame rate, audio stream). A failed check is also shown as a warning on the node. |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `filenames` | `STRING` | Full paths of the written file(s), one per line (empty in 'preview first' mode). |
| 1 | `preview` | `IMAGE` | The saved frames (the last frame only when streaming a latent or previewing first). |

### VideoInfoC2C

**Shown in the menu as:** Video Info (C2C)

Read a loader's video_info as numbers: the source file's frame rate, frame count, duration (s) and size, and the same for what was actually loaded (after skipping, caps and resizing).

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_video/nodes_load.py`  
**Provenance:** ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load — HallettVisual (Matthew Hallett) (https://github.com/HallettVisual/ComfyUI-4K-Video-Save-Load) - per CREDITS.md

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `video_info` | `VHS_VIDEOINFO` |  | Metadata from a C2C or VHS loader. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `video` | `C2C_VIDEO` |  | Optional: the loader's video output. With it, timeline adds the time base, measured frame rate, variable frame rate and audio streams, and integrity checks every frame of the source (packet timestamps of a movie - demux only, nothing decoded; numbers and headers of a sequence). |

**Outputs**

| # | Name | Type | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `source_fps` | `FLOAT` | Source frame rate. |
| 1 | `source_frame_count` | `INT` | Frames in the source. |
| 2 | `source_duration` | `FLOAT` | Source duration in seconds. |
| 3 | `source_width` | `INT` | Source width in pixels. |
| 4 | `source_height` | `INT` | Source height in pixels. |
| 5 | `loaded_fps` | `FLOAT` | Frame rate of the loaded frames. |
| 6 | `loaded_frame_count` | `INT` | Frames loaded. |
| 7 | `loaded_duration` | `FLOAT` | Duration of the loaded frames in seconds. |
| 8 | `loaded_width` | `INT` | Loaded width in pixels. |
| 9 | `loaded_height` | `INT` | Loaded height in pixels. |
| 10 | `timecode` | `STRING` | Timecode of the first loaded frame (else the source's start timecode; empty when it has none). |
| 11 | `timeline` | `STRING` | JSON: time base, start, nominal and measured frame rate, variable frame rate, frame duration range, timecode (start, end, drop frame, first / last loaded frame) and every audio stream. Without video: what video_info holds. |
| 12 | `integrity` | `STRING` | JSON: missing, duplicated, irregular or unreadable frames of the source (movie: packet timestamps; sequence: frame numbers and headers), and which fall in the loaded frames. Needs video. |
| 13 | `frames_ok` | `BOOLEAN` | True only when video is connected and the integrity check found nothing. |

## 🐺 C2C/🧰 Core/Viewer helpers


### C2CViewerDetect

**Shown in the menu as:** Viewer helper: Detect

Viewer helper (hidden): finds objects on one frame (text, points or boxes) for the Mask + Matting viewer and writes one mask per object. Not meant to be added by hand.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_viewer/detect.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | The plate. |
| `frame` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Frame to look at. |
| `model` | choice: `MISSING: sam3.1_multiplex_fp16.safetensors` | default `"MISSING: sam3.1_multiplex_fp16.safetensors"` | SAM checkpoint for Find and clicks, strongest first (SAM 3.1 Multiplex > 3.1 > 3), from models/sam3.1 and models/sam3. |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `text` | `STRING` | default `""` | What to find, comma-separated (person, dog). |
| `points` | `STRING` | default `""` | Clicks: [{"x":0.4,"y":0.5,"label":1}] in 0..1. |
| `boxes` | `STRING` | default `""` | Boxes: [[x0,y0,x1,y1]] in 0..1. |
| `max_side` | `INT` | default `960`, range 64…2048, step 16 | Longest side of the object masks the viewer gets, in pixels (detection itself runs on the full frame). |


---

### C2CViewerPlateProxy

**Shown in the menu as:** Viewer helper: Plate Proxy

Viewer helper (hidden): writes the plate as proxy frames (at most 960 px, at most 240 frames) for the Mask + Matting viewer. Not meant to be added by hand.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_viewer/plate_proxy.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | The plate (what feeds Mask + Matting's image input). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max_side` | `INT` | default `960`, range 64…2048, step 16 | Long side of the proxy frames in pixels. |
| `max_frames` | `INT` | default `240`, range 1…2000 | At most this many frames; a longer clip shows every k-th. |


---

### C2CViewerResult

**Shown in the menu as:** Viewer helper: Result

Viewer helper (hidden): writes the rendered alpha and the plate as proxy frames and measures QC for the Mask + Matting viewer. Not meant to be added by hand.

**Source:** `ComfyUI-CustomNodePacks/nodes/c2c_viewer/result.py`  
**Provenance:** none named (no PORTED FROM header, no CREDITS / NOTICE section names this file)

**Required inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image` | `IMAGE` |  | The whole plate (the rendered frames are cut from it). |
| `alpha` | `MASK` |  | Mask + Matting's alpha. |
| `start` | `INT` | default `0`, range 0…1000000 | Plate frame of the first rendered frame (the preview chunk's start). |

**Optional inputs**

| Parameter | Type | Constraints | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coarse` | `MASK` |  | Mask + Matting's mask (the segmenter's coarse selection). |
| `info` | `STRING` |  | Mask + Matting's info output (names the frames a run left empty). |
| `max_side` | `INT` | default `960`, range 64…2048, step 16 | Longest side of the preview images the viewer gets, in pixels (display only - the matte itself is untouched). |
| `max_frames` | `INT` | default `120`, range 1…2000 | Most frames sent to the viewer; longer renders are sampled evenly. |
